     1→# Entire - CLI 
     2→
     3→This repo contains the CLI for Entire.
     4→
     5→## Architecture
     6→
     7→- CLI build with github.com/spf13/cobra and github.com/charmbracelet/huh 
     8→
     9→## Key Directories
    10→
    11→### Commands (`cmd/`)
    12→- `entire/`: Main CLI entry point
    13→- `entire/cli`: CLI utilities and helpers
    14→- `entire/cli/commands`: actual command implementations
    15→- `entire/cli/strategy`: strategy implementations - see section below
    16→- `entire/cli/checkpoint`: checkpoint storage abstractions (temporary and committed)
    17→- `entire/cli/session`: session state management
    18→- `entire/cli/integration_test`: integration tests
    19→
    20→## Tech Stack
    21→
    22→- Language: Go 1.25.x
    23→- Build tool: mise, go modules
    24→- Linting: golangci-lint
    25→
    26→## Development
    27→
    28→### Running Tests
    29→```bash
    30→mise run test
    31→```
    32→
    33→### Running Integration Tests
    34→```bash
    35→mise run test:integration
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Running All Tests (CI)
    39→```bash
    40→mise run test:ci
    41→```
    42→
    43→Integration tests use the `//go:build integration` build tag and are located in `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/`.
    44→
    45→### Test Parallelization
    46→
    47→**Always use `t.Parallel()` in tests.** Every top-level test function and subtest should call `t.Parallel()` unless it modifies process-global state (e.g., `os.Chdir()`).
    48→
    49→```go
    50→func TestFeature_Foo(t *testing.T) {
    51→    t.Parallel()
    52→    // ...
    53→}
    54→
    55→// Integration tests: RunForAllStrategies handles t.Parallel() for subtests internally,
    56→// but the top-level test still needs it
    57→func TestFeature_Bar(t *testing.T) {
    58→    t.Parallel()
    59→    RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
    60→        // ...
    61→    })
    62→}
    63→```
    64→
    65→**Exception:** Tests that modify process-global state cannot be parallelized. This includes `os.Chdir()`/`t.Chdir()` and `os.Setenv()`/`t.Setenv()` — Go's test framework will panic if these are used after `t.Parallel()`.
    66→
    67→### Linting and Formatting
    68→```bash
    69→mise run fmt && mise run lint
    70→```
    71→
    72→### Before Every Commit (REQUIRED)
    73→
    74→**CI will fail if you skip these steps:**
    75→
    76→```bash
    77→mise run fmt      # Format code (CI enforces gofmt)
    78→mise run lint     # Lint check (CI enforces golangci-lint)
    79→mise run test:ci  # Run all tests (unit + integration)
    80→```
    81→
    82→Or combined: `mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci`
    83→
    84→**Common CI failures from skipping this:**
    85→- `gofmt` formatting differences → run `mise run fmt`
    86→- Lint errors → run `mise run lint` and fix issues
    87→- Test failures → run `mise run test` and fix
    88→
    89→### Code Duplication Prevention
    90→
    91→Before implementing Go code, use `/go:discover-related` to find existing utilities and patterns that might be reusable.
    92→
    93→**Check for duplication:**
    94→```bash
    95→mise run dup           # Comprehensive check (threshold 50) with summary
    96→mise run dup:staged    # Check only staged files
    97→mise run lint          # Normal lint includes dupl at threshold 75 (new issues only)
    98→mise run lint:full     # All issues at threshold 75
    99→```
   100→

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"context"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"os"
     9→	"os/exec"
    10→	"path/filepath"
    11→	"runtime"
    12→	"testing"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// testBinaryPath holds the path to the CLI binary built once in TestMain.
    16→// All tests share this binary to avoid repeated builds.
    17→var testBinaryPath string
    18→
    19→// defaultAgent holds the agent to test with, determined in TestMain.
    20→var defaultAgent string
    21→
    22→// TestMain builds the CLI binary once and checks agent availability before running tests.
    23→func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
    24→	// Determine which agent to test with
    25→	defaultAgent = os.Getenv("E2E_AGENT")
    26→	if defaultAgent == "" {
    27→		defaultAgent = AgentNameClaudeCode
    28→	}
    29→
    30→	// Check if the agent is available
    31→	runner := NewAgentRunner(defaultAgent, AgentRunnerConfig{})
    32→	available, err := runner.IsAvailable()
    33→	if !available {
    34→		fmt.Printf("Agent %s not available (%v), skipping E2E tests\n", defaultAgent, err)
    35→		// Exit 0 to not fail CI when agent isn't configured
    36→		os.Exit(0)
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	// Build binary once to a temp directory
    40→	tmpDir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "entire-e2e-test-*")
    41→	if err != nil {
    42→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to create temp dir for binary: %v\n", err)
    43→		os.Exit(1)
    44→	}
    45→
    46→	testBinaryPath = filepath.Join(tmpDir, "entire")
    47→
    48→	moduleRoot := findModuleRoot()
    49→	ctx := context.Background()
    50→
    51→	buildCmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "go", "build", "-o", testBinaryPath, ".")
    52→	buildCmd.Dir = filepath.Join(moduleRoot, "cmd", "entire")
    53→
    54→	buildOutput, err := buildCmd.CombinedOutput()
    55→	if err != nil {
    56→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to build CLI binary: %v\nOutput: %s\n", err, buildOutput)
    57→		os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    58→		os.Exit(1)
    59→	}
    60→
    61→	// Add binary to PATH so hooks can find it.
    62→	// This is safe because:
    63→	// 1. TestMain runs once before any tests (no parallel conflict with os.Setenv)
    64→	// 2. Each test package gets its own TestMain execution
    65→	// 3. PATH is restored in the same TestMain after m.Run() completes
    66→	// 4. The binary path is unique per test run (temp dir)
    67→	origPath := os.Getenv("PATH")
    68→	os.Setenv("PATH", tmpDir+string(os.PathListSeparator)+origPath)
    69→
    70→	// Run tests
    71→	code := m.Run()
    72→
    73→	// Cleanup
    74→	os.Setenv("PATH", origPath)
    75→	os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    76→	os.Exit(code)
    77→}
    78→
    79→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.
    80→// It panics if TestMain hasn't run (testBinaryPath is empty).
    81→func getTestBinary() string {
    82→	if testBinaryPath == "" {
    83→		panic("testBinaryPath not set - TestMain must run before tests")
    84→	}
    85→	return testBinaryPath
    86→}
    87→
    88→// findModuleRoot finds the Go module root by walking up from the current file.
    89→func findModuleRoot() string {
    90→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
    91→	if !ok {
    92→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
    93→	}
    94→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
    95→
    96→	for {
    97→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
    98→			return dir
    99→		}
   100→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
   101→		if parent == dir {
   102→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
   103→		}
   104→		dir = parent
   105→	}
   106→}
   107→

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"bytes"
     7→	"context"
     8→	"errors"
     9→	"fmt"
    10→	"os"
    11→	"os/exec"
    12→	"time"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// AgentNameClaudeCode is the name for Claude Code agent.
    16→const AgentNameClaudeCode = "claude-code"
    17→
    18→// AgentNameGeminiCLI is the name for Gemini CLI agent.
    19→const AgentNameGeminiCLI = "gemini-cli"
    20→
    21→// AgentRunner abstracts invoking a coding agent for e2e tests.
    22→// This follows the multi-agent pattern from cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go.
    23→type AgentRunner interface {
    24→	// Name returns the agent name (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini-cli")
    25→	Name() string
    26→
    27→	// IsAvailable checks if the agent CLI is installed and authenticated
    28→	IsAvailable() (bool, error)
    29→
    30→	// RunPrompt executes a prompt and returns the result
    31→	RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error)
    32→
    33→	// RunPromptWithTools executes with specific allowed tools
    34→	RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error)
    35→}
    36→
    37→// AgentResult holds the result of an agent invocation.
    38→type AgentResult struct {
    39→	Stdout   string
    40→	Stderr   string
    41→	ExitCode int
    42→	Duration time.Duration
    43→}
    44→
    45→// AgentRunnerConfig holds configuration for agent runners.
    46→type AgentRunnerConfig struct {
    47→	Model   string        // Model to use (e.g., "haiku" for Claude)
    48→	Timeout time.Duration // Timeout per prompt
    49→}
    50→
    51→// NewAgentRunner creates an agent runner based on the agent name.
    52→//
    53→//nolint:ireturn // factory pattern intentionally returns interface
    54→func NewAgentRunner(name string, config AgentRunnerConfig) AgentRunner {
    55→	switch name {
    56→	case AgentNameClaudeCode:
    57→		return NewClaudeCodeRunner(config)
    58→	case AgentNameGeminiCLI:
    59→		return NewGeminiCLIRunner(config)
    60→	default:
    61→		// Return a runner that reports as unavailable
    62→		return &unavailableRunner{name: name}
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// unavailableRunner is returned for unknown agent names.
    67→type unavailableRunner struct {
    68→	name string
    69→}
    70→
    71→func (r *unavailableRunner) Name() string { return r.name }
    72→
    73→func (r *unavailableRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
    74→	return false, fmt.Errorf("unknown agent: %s", r.name)
    75→}
    76→
    77→func (r *unavailableRunner) RunPrompt(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string) (*AgentResult, error) {
    78→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent %s is not available", r.name)
    79→}
    80→
    81→func (r *unavailableRunner) RunPromptWithTools(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string, _ []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
    82→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent %s is not available", r.name)
    83→}
    84→
    85→// ClaudeCodeRunner implements AgentRunner for Claude Code CLI.
    86→type ClaudeCodeRunner struct {
    87→	Model        string
    88→	Timeout      time.Duration
    89→	AllowedTools []string
    90→}
    91→
    92→// NewClaudeCodeRunner creates a new Claude Code runner with the given config.
    93→func NewClaudeCodeRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *ClaudeCodeRunner {
    94→	model := config.Model
    95→	if model == "" {
    96→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL")
    97→		if model == "" {
    98→			model = "haiku"
    99→		}
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	timeout := config.Timeout
   103→	if timeout == 0 {
   104→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   105→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   106→				timeout = parsed
   107→			}
   108→		}
   109→		if timeout == 0 {
   110→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   111→		}
   112→	}
   113→
   114→	return &ClaudeCodeRunner{
   115→		Model:        model,
   116→		Timeout:      timeout,
   117→		AllowedTools: []string{"Edit", "Read", "Write", "Bash", "Glob", "Grep"},
   118→	}
   119→}
   120→
   121→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) Name() string {
   122→	return AgentNameClaudeCode
   123→}
   124→
   125→// IsAvailable checks if Claude CLI is installed and responds to --version.
   126→// Note: This does NOT verify authentication status. Claude Code uses OAuth
   127→// authentication (via `claude login`), not ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. If the CLI is
   128→// installed but not logged in, tests will fail at RunPrompt time.
   129→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   130→	// Check if claude CLI is in PATH
   131→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("claude"); err != nil {
   132→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   133→	}
   134→
   135→	// Check if claude is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   136→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   137→	defer cancel()
   138→
   139→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", "--version")
   140→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   141→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not working: %w", err)
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	return true, nil
   145→}
   146→
   147→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   148→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, r.AllowedTools)
   149→}
   150→
   151→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   152→	// Build command: claude --model <model> -p "<prompt>" --allowedTools <tools>
   153→	args := []string{
   154→		"--model", r.Model,
   155→		"-p", prompt,
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	if len(tools) > 0 {
   159→		// Claude CLI expects each tool as a separate argument after --allowedTools
   160→		// e.g., --allowedTools Edit Read Bash (not --allowedTools "Edit,Read,Bash")
   161→		args = append(args, "--allowedTools")
   162→		args = append(args, tools...)
   163→	}
   164→
   165→	// Create context with timeout
   166→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   167→	defer cancel()
   168→
   169→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   170→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", args...)
   171→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   172→
   173→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   174→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   175→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   176→
   177→	start := time.Now()
   178→	err := cmd.Run()
   179→	duration := time.Since(start)
   180→
   181→	result := &AgentResult{
   182→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   183→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   184→		Duration: duration,
   185→	}
   186→
   187→	if err != nil {
   188→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   189→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   190→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   191→		} else {
   192→			result.ExitCode = -1
   193→		}
   194→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   195→		return result, err
   196→	}
   197→
   198→	result.ExitCode = 0
   199→	return result, nil
   200→}
   201→
   202→// GeminiCLIRunner implements AgentRunner for Gemini CLI.
   203→// This is a placeholder for future implementation.
   204→type GeminiCLIRunner struct {
   205→	Timeout time.Duration
   206→}
   207→
   208→// NewGeminiCLIRunner creates a new Gemini CLI runner with the given config.
   209→func NewGeminiCLIRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *GeminiCLIRunner {
   210→	timeout := config.Timeout
   211→	if timeout == 0 {
   212→		timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   213→	}
   214→
   215→	return &GeminiCLIRunner{
   216→		Timeout: timeout,
   217→	}
   218→}
   219→
   220→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) Name() string {
   221→	return AgentNameGeminiCLI
   222→}
   223→
   224→// IsAvailable checks if Gemini CLI is installed and authenticated.
   225→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   226→	// Check if gemini CLI is in PATH
   227→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("gemini"); err != nil {
   228→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   229→	}
   230→
   231→	// Check if gemini is working
   232→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   233→	defer cancel()
   234→
   235→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", "--version")
   236→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   237→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not working: %w", err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	return true, nil
   241→}
   242→
   243→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   244→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, nil)
   245→}
   246→
   247→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string, _ []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   248→	// Gemini CLI implementation would go here
   249→	// For now, return an error indicating it's not fully implemented
   250→	return nil, errors.New("gemini CLI runner not yet implemented")
   251→}
   252→

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"regexp"
     7→	"strings"
     8→	"testing"
     9→)
    10→
    11→// AssertFileContains checks that a file contains the expected substring.
    12→func AssertFileContains(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, path, expected string) {
    13→	t.Helper()
    14→
    15→	content := env.ReadFile(path)
    16→	if !strings.Contains(content, expected) {
    17→		t.Errorf("File %s does not contain expected string.\nExpected substring: %q\nActual content:\n%s",
    18→			path, expected, content)
    19→	}
    20→}
    21→
    22→// AssertFileMatches checks that a file content matches a regex pattern.
    23→func AssertFileMatches(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, path, pattern string) {
    24→	t.Helper()
    25→
    26→	content := env.ReadFile(path)
    27→	matched, err := regexp.MatchString(pattern, content)
    28→	if err != nil {
    29→		t.Fatalf("Invalid regex pattern %q: %v", pattern, err)
    30→	}
    31→	if !matched {
    32→		t.Errorf("File %s does not match pattern.\nPattern: %q\nActual content:\n%s",
    33→			path, pattern, content)
    34→	}
    35→}
    36→
    37→// AssertHelloWorldProgram verifies a Go file is a valid hello world program.
    38→// Uses flexible matching to handle agent variations.
    39→func AssertHelloWorldProgram(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, path string) {
    40→	t.Helper()
    41→
    42→	content := env.ReadFile(path)
    43→
    44→	// Check for package main
    45→	if !strings.Contains(content, "package main") {
    46→		t.Errorf("File %s missing 'package main'", path)
    47→	}
    48→
    49→	// Check for main function (flexible whitespace)
    50→	mainFuncPattern := regexp.MustCompile(`func\s+main\s*\(\s*\)`)
    51→	if !mainFuncPattern.MatchString(content) {
    52→		t.Errorf("File %s missing main function", path)
    53→	}
    54→
    55→	// Check for Hello, World! (case insensitive)
    56→	helloPattern := regexp.MustCompile(`(?i)hello.+world`)
    57→	if !helloPattern.MatchString(content) {
    58→		t.Errorf("File %s missing 'Hello, World!' output", path)
    59→	}
    60→}
    61→
    62→// AssertCalculatorFunctions verifies calc.go has the expected functions.
    63→func AssertCalculatorFunctions(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, path string, functions ...string) {
    64→	t.Helper()
    65→
    66→	content := env.ReadFile(path)
    67→
    68→	for _, fn := range functions {
    69→		// Check for function definition (flexible whitespace)
    70→		pattern := regexp.MustCompile(`func\s+` + regexp.QuoteMeta(fn) + `\s*\(`)
    71→		if !pattern.MatchString(content) {
    72→			t.Errorf("File %s missing function %s", path, fn)
    73→		}
    74→	}
    75→}
    76→
    77→// AssertRewindPointExists checks that at least one rewind point exists.
    78→func AssertRewindPointExists(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv) {
    79→	t.Helper()
    80→
    81→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    82→	if len(points) == 0 {
    83→		t.Error("Expected at least one rewind point, but none exist")
    84→	}
    85→}
    86→
    87→// AssertRewindPointCount checks that the expected number of rewind points exist.
    88→func AssertRewindPointCount(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, expected int) {
    89→	t.Helper()
    90→
    91→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    92→	if len(points) != expected {
    93→		t.Errorf("Expected %d rewind points, got %d", expected, len(points))
    94→		for i, p := range points {
    95→			t.Logf("  Point %d: ID=%s, Message=%s", i, p.ID, p.Message)
    96→		}
    97→	}
    98→}
    99→
   100→// AssertRewindPointCountAtLeast checks that at least the expected number of rewind points exist.
   101→func AssertRewindPointCountAtLeast(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, minimum int) {
   102→	t.Helper()
   103→
   104→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
   105→	if len(points) < minimum {
   106→		t.Errorf("Expected at least %d rewind points, got %d", minimum, len(points))
   107→		for i, p := range points {
   108→			t.Logf("  Point %d: ID=%s, Message=%s", i, p.ID, p.Message)
   109→		}
   110→	}
   111→}
   112→
   113→// AssertCheckpointExists checks that a checkpoint trailer exists in commit history.
   114→func AssertCheckpointExists(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv) {
   115→	t.Helper()
   116→
   117→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
   118→	if err != nil || checkpointID == "" {
   119→		t.Errorf("Expected checkpoint trailer in commit history, but none found: %v", err)
   120→	}
   121→}
   122→
   123→// AssertBranchExists checks that a branch exists.
   124→func AssertBranchExists(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, branchName string) {
   125→	t.Helper()
   126→
   127→	if !env.BranchExists(branchName) {
   128→		t.Errorf("Expected branch %s to exist, but it doesn't", branchName)
   129→	}
   130→}
   131→
   132→// AssertAgentSuccess checks that an agent result indicates success.
   133→func AssertAgentSuccess(t *testing.T, result *AgentResult, err error) {
   134→	t.Helper()
   135→
   136→	if err != nil {
   137→		stderr := ""
   138→		if result != nil {
   139→			stderr = result.Stderr
   140→		}
   141→		t.Errorf("Agent failed with error: %v\nStderr: %s", err, stderr)
   142→	}
   143→	if result != nil && result.ExitCode != 0 {
   144→		t.Errorf("Agent exited with code %d\nStdout: %s\nStderr: %s",
   145→			result.ExitCode, result.Stdout, result.Stderr)
   146→	}
   147→}
   148→
   149→// AssertExpectedFilesExist checks that all expected files from a prompt template exist.
   150→func AssertExpectedFilesExist(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, prompt PromptTemplate) {
   151→	t.Helper()
   152→
   153→	for _, file := range prompt.ExpectedFiles {
   154→		if !env.FileExists(file) {
   155→			t.Errorf("Expected file %s to exist after prompt %s, but it doesn't", file, prompt.Name)
   156→		}
   157→	}
   158→}

     1→//go:build e2e

     5→// PromptTemplate defines a deterministic prompt with expected outcomes.
     6→type PromptTemplate struct {
     7→	Name          string   // Unique name for the prompt
     8→	Prompt        string   // The actual prompt text
     9→	ExpectedFiles []string // Files expected to be created/modified
    10→}
    11→
    12→// Deterministic prompts designed for predictable outcomes with minimal token usage.
    13→
    14→// PromptCreateHelloGo creates a simple Go hello world program.
    15→var PromptCreateHelloGo = PromptTemplate{
    16→	Name: "CreateHelloGo",
    17→	Prompt: `Create a file called hello.go with a simple Go program that prints "Hello, World!".
    18→Requirements:
    19→- Use package main
    20→- Use a main function
    21→- Use fmt.Println to print exactly "Hello, World!"
    22→- Do not add comments, tests, or extra functionality
    23→- Do not create any other files`,
    24→	ExpectedFiles: []string{"hello.go"},
    25→}
    26→
    27→// PromptModifyHelloGo modifies the hello.go file to print a different message.
    28→var PromptModifyHelloGo = PromptTemplate{
    29→	Name: "ModifyHelloGo",
    30→	Prompt: `Modify hello.go to print "Hello, E2E Test!" instead of "Hello, World!".
    31→Do not add any other functionality or files.`,
    32→	ExpectedFiles: []string{"hello.go"},
    33→}
    34→
    35→// PromptCreateCalculator creates a simple calculator with add/subtract functions.
    36→var PromptCreateCalculator = PromptTemplate{
    37→	Name: "CreateCalculator",
    38→	Prompt: `Create a file called calc.go with two exported functions:
    39→- Add(a, b int) int - returns a + b
    40→- Subtract(a, b int) int - returns a - b
    41→Requirements:
    42→- Use package main
    43→- No comments or documentation
    44→- No main function
    45→- No tests
    46→- No other files`,
    47→	ExpectedFiles: []string{"calc.go"},
    48→}
    49→
    50→// PromptCreateConfig creates a simple JSON config file.
    51→var PromptCreateConfig = PromptTemplate{
    52→	Name: "CreateConfig",
    53→	Prompt: `Create a file called config.json with this exact content:
    54→{
    55→  "name": "e2e-test",
    56→  "version": "1.0.0",
    57→  "enabled": true
    58→}
    59→Do not create any other files.`,
    60→	ExpectedFiles: []string{"config.json"},
    61→}
    62→
    63→// PromptAddMultiplyFunction adds a multiply function to calc.go.
    64→var PromptAddMultiplyFunction = PromptTemplate{
    65→	Name: "AddMultiplyFunction",
    66→	Prompt: `Add a Multiply function to calc.go that multiplies two integers.
    67→- Signature: Multiply(a, b int) int
    68→- No comments
    69→- No other changes`,
    70→	ExpectedFiles: []string{"calc.go"},
    71→}
    72→
    73→// PromptCreateDocs creates a simple DOCS.md documentation file.
    74→// Note: Uses DOCS.md instead of README.md to avoid conflicts with
    75→// the README.md created by NewFeatureBranchEnv during test setup.
    76→var PromptCreateDocs = PromptTemplate{
    77→	Name: "CreateDocs",
    78→	Prompt: `Create a file called DOCS.md with exactly this content:
    79→# Documentation
    80→
    81→This is the documentation file.
    82→
    83→## Usage
    84→
    85→Run the program with: go run .
    86→`,
    87→	ExpectedFiles: []string{"DOCS.md"},
    88→}
    89→
    90→// PromptCommitChanges instructs the agent to commit changes.

    95→Use git add and git commit commands.`,
    96→	ExpectedFiles: []string{},
    97→}

    23→
    24→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for E2E tests with real agent calls.
    25→type TestEnv struct {
    26→	T       *testing.T
    27→	RepoDir string
    28→	Agent   AgentRunner
    29→}
    30→
    31→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated E2E test environment.
    32→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    33→	t.Helper()
    34→
    35→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    36→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    37→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    38→		repoDir = resolved
    39→	}
    40→
    41→	// Create agent runner
    42→	agent := NewAgentRunner(defaultAgent, AgentRunnerConfig{})
    43→
    44→	return &TestEnv{
    45→		T:       t,
    46→		RepoDir: repoDir,
    47→		Agent:   agent,
    48→	}
    49→}
    50→
    51→// NewFeatureBranchEnv creates an E2E test environment ready for testing.
    52→// It initializes the repo, creates an initial commit on main,
    53→// checks out a feature branch, and sets up agent hooks.
    54→func NewFeatureBranchEnv(t *testing.T, strategyName string) *TestEnv {
    55→	t.Helper()
    56→
    57→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    58→	env.InitRepo()
    59→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository\n\nThis is a test repository for E2E testing.\n")
    60→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
    61→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
    62→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/e2e-test")
    63→
    64→	// Use `entire enable` to set up everything (hooks, settings, etc.)
    65→	// This sets up .entire/settings.json and .claude/settings.json with hooks
    66→	env.RunEntireEnable(strategyName)
    67→
    68→	// Commit all files created by `entire enable` so they survive git stash -u operations.
    69→	// Without this, stash operations would stash away the hooks config and entire settings,
    70→	// causing hooks to not fire for subsequent prompts and stash pop conflicts.
    71→	// Using "git add ." is safe in test repos since we control all content.
    72→	env.GitAddAll()
    73→	env.GitCommit("Add entire and agent config")
    74→
    75→	return env
    76→}
    77→
    78→// RunEntireEnable runs `entire enable` to set up the project with hooks.
    79→// Uses the configured defaultAgent (from E2E_AGENT env var or "claude-code").
    80→func (env *TestEnv) RunEntireEnable(strategyName string) {
    81→	env.T.Helper()
    82→
    83→	args := []string{
    84→		"enable",
    85→		"--agent", defaultAgent,
    86→		"--strategy", strategyName,
    87→		"--telemetry=false",
    88→		"--force", // Force reinstall hooks in case they exist
    89→	}
    90→
    91→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are static
    92→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), args...)
    93→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    94→
    95→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
    96→	if err != nil {
    97→		env.T.Fatalf("entire enable failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
    98→	}
    99→	env.T.Logf("entire enable output: %s", output)
   100→}
   101→
   102→// InitRepo initializes a git repository in the test environment.
   103→func (env *TestEnv) InitRepo() {
   104→	env.T.Helper()
   105→
   106→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(env.RepoDir, false)
   107→	if err != nil {
   108→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to init git repo: %v", err)
   109→	}
   110→
   111→	// Configure git user for commits
   112→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   113→	if err != nil {
   114→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   115→	}
   116→	cfg.User.Name = "E2E Test User"
   117→	cfg.User.Email = "e2e-test@example.com"
   118→
   119→	// Disable GPG signing for test commits
   120→	if cfg.Raw == nil {
   121→		cfg.Raw = config.New()
   122→	}
   123→	cfg.Raw.Section("commit").SetOption("gpgsign", "false")
   124→
   125→	if err := repo.SetConfig(cfg); err != nil {
   126→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to set repo config: %v", err)
   127→	}
   128→}
   129→
   130→// WriteFile creates a file with the given content in the test repo.
   131→func (env *TestEnv) WriteFile(path, content string) {
   132→	env.T.Helper()
   133→
   134→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   135→
   136→	// Create parent directories
   137→	dir := filepath.Dir(fullPath)
   138→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   139→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
   140→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to create directory %s: %v", dir, err)
   141→	}
   142→
   143→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   144→	if err := os.WriteFile(fullPath, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   145→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write file %s: %v", path, err)
   146→	}
   147→}
   148→
   149→// ReadFile reads a file from the test repo.
   150→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFile(path string) string {
   151→	env.T.Helper()
   152→
   153→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   154→	//nolint:gosec // test code, path is from test setup
   155→	data, err := os.ReadFile(fullPath)
   156→	if err != nil {
   157→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read file %s: %v", path, err)
   158→	}
   159→	return string(data)
   160→}
   161→
   162→// TryReadFile reads a file from the test repo, returning empty string if not found.
   163→func (env *TestEnv) TryReadFile(path string) string {
   164→	env.T.Helper()
   165→
   166→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   167→	//nolint:gosec // test code, path is from test setup
   168→	data, err := os.ReadFile(fullPath)
   169→	if err != nil {
   170→		return ""
   171→	}
   172→	return string(data)
   173→}
   174→
   175→// FileExists checks if a file exists in the test repo.
   176→func (env *TestEnv) FileExists(path string) bool {
   177→	env.T.Helper()
   178→
   179→	fullPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, path)
   180→	_, err := os.Stat(fullPath)
   181→	return err == nil
   182→}
   183→
   184→// GitAdd stages files for commit.
   185→func (env *TestEnv) GitAdd(paths ...string) {
   186→	env.T.Helper()
   187→
   188→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   189→	if err != nil {
   190→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   191→	}
   192→
   193→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   194→	if err != nil {
   195→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   196→	}
   197→
   198→	for _, path := range paths {
   199→		if _, err := worktree.Add(path); err != nil {
   200→			env.T.Fatalf("failed to add file %s: %v", path, err)
   201→		}
   202→	}
   203→}
   204→
   205→// GitAddAll stages all files (git add .).
   206→func (env *TestEnv) GitAddAll() {
   207→	env.T.Helper()
   208→
   209→	//nolint:gosec // test code, "." is safe
   210→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "add", ".")
   211→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   212→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   213→		env.T.Fatalf("git add . failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   214→	}
   215→}
   216→
   217→// GitCommit creates a commit with all staged files.
   218→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommit(message string) {
   219→	env.T.Helper()
   220→
   221→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   222→	if err != nil {
   223→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   224→	}
   225→
   226→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   227→	if err != nil {
   228→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   229→	}
   230→
   231→	_, err = worktree.Commit(message, &git.CommitOptions{
   232→		Author: &object.Signature{
   233→			Name:  "E2E Test User",
   234→			Email: "e2e-test@example.com",
   235→			When:  time.Now(),
   236→		},
   237→	})
   238→	if err != nil {
   239→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   240→	}
   241→}
   242→
   243→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, running the prepare-commit-msg
   244→// and post-commit hooks like a real workflow.
   245→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   246→	env.T.Helper()
   247→
   248→	// Stage files using go-git
   249→	for _, file := range files {
   250→		env.GitAdd(file)
   251→	}
   252→
   253→	// Create a temp file for the commit message
   254→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   255→	//nolint:gosec // test code, permissions are intentionally standard
   256→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   257→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   258→	}
   259→
   260→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook
   261→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   262→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")
   263→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   264→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   265→		"ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1",
   266→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   267→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   268→	)
   269→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   270→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   271→	}
   272→
   273→	// Read the modified message
   274→	//nolint:gosec // test code, path is from test setup
   275→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   276→	if err != nil {
   277→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   278→	}
   279→
   280→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
   281→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   282→	if err != nil {
   283→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   284→	}
   285→
   286→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   287→	if err != nil {
   288→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   289→	}
   290→
   291→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
   292→		Author: &object.Signature{
   293→			Name:  "E2E Test User",
   294→			Email: "e2e-test@example.com",
   295→			When:  time.Now(),
   296→		},
   297→	})
   298→	if err != nil {
   299→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	// Run post-commit hook
   303→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are from trusted test setup, no context needed
   304→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   305→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   306→	postCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   307→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".claude"),
   308→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".gemini"),
   309→	)
   310→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   311→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   312→	}
   313→}
   314→
   315→// GitCheckoutNewBranch creates and checks out a new branch.
   316→func (env *TestEnv) GitCheckoutNewBranch(branchName string) {
   317→	env.T.Helper()
   318→
   319→	//nolint:noctx // test code, no context needed for git checkout
   320→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "checkout", "-b", branchName)
   321→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   322→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   323→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to checkout new branch %s: %v\nOutput: %s", branchName, err, output)
   324→	}
   325→}
   326→
   327→// GetHeadHash returns the current HEAD commit hash.
   328→func (env *TestEnv) GetHeadHash() string {
   329→	env.T.Helper()
   330→
   331→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   332→	if err != nil {
   333→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   334→	}
   335→
   336→	head, err := repo.Head()
   337→	if err != nil {
   338→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   339→	}
   340→
   341→	return head.Hash().String()
   342→}
   343→
   344→// RewindPoint mirrors the rewind --list JSON output.
   345→type RewindPoint struct {
   346→	ID               string    `json:"id"`
   347→	Message          string    `json:"message"`

   356→// GetRewindPoints returns available rewind points using the CLI.
   357→func (env *TestEnv) GetRewindPoints() []RewindPoint {
   358→	env.T.Helper()
   359→
   360→	//nolint:gosec,noctx // test code, args are static, no context needed
   361→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "rewind", "--list")
   362→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   363→
   364→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   365→	if err != nil {
   366→		env.T.Fatalf("rewind --list failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   367→	}
   368→
   369→	// Parse JSON output
   370→	var jsonPoints []struct {
   371→		ID               string `json:"id"`
   372→		Message          string `json:"message"`
   373→		MetadataDir      string `json:"metadata_dir"`
   374→		Date             string `json:"date"`
   375→		IsTaskCheckpoint bool   `json:"is_task_checkpoint"`
   376→		ToolUseID        string `json:"tool_use_id"`
   377→		IsLogsOnly       bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
   378→		CondensationID   string `json:"condensation_id"`
   379→	}
   380→
   381→	if err := json.Unmarshal(output, &jsonPoints); err != nil {
   382→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to parse rewind points: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   383→	}
   384→
   385→	points := make([]RewindPoint, len(jsonPoints))
   386→	for i, jp := range jsonPoints {
   387→		//nolint:errcheck // date parsing failure is acceptable, defaults to zero time
   388→		date, _ := time.Parse(time.RFC3339, jp.Date)
   389→		points[i] = RewindPoint{
   390→			ID:               jp.ID,
   391→			Message:          jp.Message,
   392→			MetadataDir:      jp.MetadataDir,
   393→			Date:             date,
   394→			IsTaskCheckpoint: jp.IsTaskCheckpoint,
   395→			ToolUseID:        jp.ToolUseID,
   396→			IsLogsOnly:       jp.IsLogsOnly,
   397→			CondensationID:   jp.CondensationID,
   398→		}
   399→	}
   400→
   401→	return points
   402→}

   465→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD and returns
   466→// the checkpoint ID from the first commit with an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
   467→// Returns an error if no checkpoint trailer is found in any commit.
   468→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() (string, error) {
   469→	env.T.Helper()
   470→
   471→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   472→	if err != nil {
   473→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   474→	}
   475→
   476→	head, err := repo.Head()
   477→	if err != nil {
   478→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
   479→	}
   480→
   481→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
   482→	if err != nil {
   483→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
   484→	}
   485→
   486→	var checkpointID string
   487→	//nolint:errcheck,gosec // ForEach callback returns error to stop iteration, not a real error
   488→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   489→		// Look for Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   490→		for _, line := range strings.Split(c.Message, "\n") {
   491→			line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
   492→			if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   493→				checkpointID = strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:"))
   494→				return errors.New("stop iteration")
   495→			}
   496→		}
   497→		return nil
   498→	})
   499→
   500→	if checkpointID == "" {
   501→		return "", errors.New("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
   502→	}
   503→
   504→	return checkpointID, nil
   505→}
   506→
   507→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the checkpoint ID from HEAD commit, or empty string if none.
   508→// This is a convenience wrapper that doesn't fail the test on missing checkpoint.
   509→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
   510→	env.T.Helper()
   511→
   512→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   513→	if err != nil {
   514→		return ""
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	head, err := repo.Head()
   518→	if err != nil {
   519→		return ""
   520→	}
   521→
   522→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   523→	if err != nil {
   524→		return ""
   525→	}
   526→
   527→	// Look for Entire-Checkpoint trailer in HEAD commit only
   528→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
   529→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
   530→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
   531→			return strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:"))
   532→		}
   533→	}
   534→	return ""
   535→}

   537→// safeIDPrefix returns first 12 chars of ID or the full ID if shorter.
   538→// Use this when logging checkpoint IDs to avoid index out of bounds panic.
   539→func safeIDPrefix(id string) string {
   540→	if len(id) >= 12 {
   541→		return id[:12]
   542→	}
   543→	return id
   544→}

   694→// ReadFileFromBranch reads a file's content from a specific branch's tree.
   695→// Returns the content and true if found, empty string and false if not found.
   696→func (env *TestEnv) ReadFileFromBranch(branchName, filePath string) (string, bool) {
   697→	env.T.Helper()
   698→
   699→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   700→	if err != nil {
   701→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   702→	}
   703→
   704→	// Get the branch reference
   705→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   706→	if err != nil {
   707→		// Try as a remote-style ref
   708→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.ReferenceName("refs/heads/"+branchName), true)
   709→		if err != nil {
   710→			return "", false
   711→		}
   712→	}
   713→
   714→	// Get the commit
   715→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   716→	if err != nil {
   717→		return "", false
   718→	}
   719→
   720→	// Get the tree
   721→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   722→	if err != nil {
   723→		return "", false
   724→	}
   725→
   726→	// Get the file
   727→	file, err := tree.File(filePath)
   728→	if err != nil {
   729→		return "", false
   730→	}
   731→
   732→	// Get the content
   733→	content, err := file.Contents()
   734→	if err != nil {
   735→		return "", false
   736→	}
   737→
   738→	return content, true
   739→}

   741→// CheckpointValidation contains expected values for checkpoint validation.
   742→type CheckpointValidation struct {
   743→	// CheckpointID is the expected checkpoint ID
   744→	CheckpointID string
   745→
   746→	// Strategy is the expected strategy name (optional)
   747→	Strategy string
   748→
   749→	// FilesTouched are the expected files in files_touched (optional)
   750→	FilesTouched []string
   751→
   752→	// ExpectedPrompts are strings that should appear in prompt.txt (optional)
   753→	ExpectedPrompts []string
   754→
   755→	// ExpectedTranscriptContent are strings that should appear in full.jsonl (optional)
   756→	ExpectedTranscriptContent []string
   757→}
   758→
   759→// ValidateCheckpoint performs comprehensive validation of a checkpoint on the metadata branch.
   760→func (env *TestEnv) ValidateCheckpoint(v CheckpointValidation) {
   761→	env.T.Helper()
   762→
   763→	metadataBranch := "entire/checkpoints/v1"
   764→
   765→	// Compute sharded path: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/
   766→	if len(v.CheckpointID) < 3 {
   767→		env.T.Fatalf("Checkpoint ID too short: %s", v.CheckpointID)
   768→	}
   769→	shardedPath := v.CheckpointID[:2] + "/" + v.CheckpointID[2:]
   770→
   771→	// Validate root metadata.json exists and has expected fields
   772→	summaryPath := shardedPath + "/metadata.json"
   773→	summaryContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, summaryPath)
   774→	if !found {
   775→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary not found at %s", summaryPath)
   776→	} else {
   777→		var summary map[string]any
   778→		if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(summaryContent), &summary); err != nil {
   779→			env.T.Errorf("Failed to parse CheckpointSummary: %v", err)
   780→		} else {
   781→			// Validate checkpoint_id
   782→			if cpID, ok := summary["checkpoint_id"].(string); !ok || cpID != v.CheckpointID {
   783→				env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.checkpoint_id = %v, want %s", summary["checkpoint_id"], v.CheckpointID)
   784→			}
   785→			// Validate strategy if specified
   786→			if v.Strategy != "" {
   787→				if strategy, ok := summary["strategy"].(string); !ok || strategy != v.Strategy {
   788→					env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.strategy = %v, want %s", summary["strategy"], v.Strategy)
   789→				}
   790→			}
   791→			// Validate sessions array exists
   792→			if sessions, ok := summary["sessions"].([]any); !ok || len(sessions) == 0 {
   793→				env.T.Error("CheckpointSummary.sessions should have at least one entry")
   794→			}
   795→			// Validate files_touched if specified
   796→			if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
   797→				if filesTouched, ok := summary["files_touched"].([]any); ok {
   798→					touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   799→					for _, f := range filesTouched {
   800→						if s, ok := f.(string); ok {
   801→							touchedSet[s] = true
   802→						}
   803→					}
   804→					for _, expected := range v.FilesTouched {
   805→						if !touchedSet[expected] {
   806→							env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.files_touched missing %q", expected)
   807→						}
   808→					}
   809→				}
   810→			}
   811→		}
   812→	}
   813→
   814→	// Validate session metadata.json exists
   815→	sessionMetadataPath := shardedPath + "/0/metadata.json"
   816→	sessionContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, sessionMetadataPath)
   817→	if !found {
   818→		env.T.Errorf("Session metadata not found at %s", sessionMetadataPath)
   819→	} else {
   820→		var metadata map[string]any
   821→		if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionContent), &metadata); err != nil {
   822→			env.T.Errorf("Failed to parse session metadata: %v", err)
   823→		} else {
   824→			// Validate checkpoint_id matches
   825→			if cpID, ok := metadata["checkpoint_id"].(string); !ok || cpID != v.CheckpointID {
   826→				env.T.Errorf("Session metadata.checkpoint_id = %v, want %s", metadata["checkpoint_id"], v.CheckpointID)
   827→			}
   828→			// Validate created_at exists
   829→			if _, ok := metadata["created_at"].(string); !ok {
   830→				env.T.Error("Session metadata.created_at should exist")
   831→			}
   832→		}
   833→	}
   834→
   835→	// Validate transcript is valid JSONL
   836→	transcriptPath := shardedPath + "/0/full.jsonl"
   837→	transcriptContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, transcriptPath)
   838→	if !found {
   839→		env.T.Errorf("Transcript not found at %s", transcriptPath)
   840→	} else {
   841→		// Check each line is valid JSON
   842→		lines := strings.Split(transcriptContent, "\n")
   843→		validLines := 0
   844→		for i, line := range lines {
   845→			line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
   846→			if line == "" {
   847→				continue
   848→			}
   849→			validLines++
   850→			var obj map[string]any
   851→			if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(line), &obj); err != nil {
   852→				env.T.Errorf("Transcript line %d is not valid JSON: %v", i+1, err)
   853→			}
   854→		}
   855→		if validLines == 0 {
   856→			env.T.Error("Transcript is empty (no valid JSONL lines)")
   857→		}
   858→		// Check expected content
   859→		for _, expected := range v.ExpectedTranscriptContent {
   860→			if !strings.Contains(transcriptContent, expected) {
   861→				env.T.Errorf("Transcript should contain %q", expected)
   862→			}
   863→		}
   864→	}
   865→
   866→	// Validate prompt.txt contains expected prompts
   867→	if len(v.ExpectedPrompts) > 0 {
   868→		promptPath := shardedPath + "/0/prompt.txt"
   869→		promptContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, promptPath)
   870→		if !found {
   871→			env.T.Errorf("Prompt file not found at %s", promptPath)
   872→		} else {
   873→			for _, expected := range v.ExpectedPrompts {
   874→				if !strings.Contains(promptContent, expected) {
   875→					env.T.Errorf("Prompt file should contain %q", expected)
   876→				}
   877→			}
   878→		}
   879→	}
   880→
   881→	// Validate content hash exists and matches transcript
   882→	hashPath := shardedPath + "/0/content_hash.txt"
   883→	hashContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(metadataBranch, hashPath)
   884→	if !found {
   885→		env.T.Errorf("Content hash not found at %s", hashPath)
   886→	} else if transcriptContent != "" {
   887→		// Verify hash matches
   888→		hash := sha256.Sum256([]byte(transcriptContent))
   889→		expectedHash := "sha256:" + hex.EncodeToString(hash[:])
   890→		storedHash := strings.TrimSpace(hashContent)
   891→		if storedHash != expectedHash {
   892→			env.T.Errorf("Content hash mismatch:\n  stored:   %s\n  expected: %s", storedHash, expectedHash)
   893→		}
   894→	}
   895→}

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"testing"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
     9→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// TestE2E_BasicWorkflow tests the fundamental workflow:
    13→// Agent creates a file -> User commits -> Checkpoint is created
    14→func TestE2E_BasicWorkflow(t *testing.T) {
    15→	t.Parallel()
    16→
    17→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    18→
    19→	// 1. Agent creates a file
    20→	t.Log("Step 1: Running agent to create hello.go")
    21→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    22→	require.NoError(t, err, "Agent should succeed")
    23→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    24→	t.Logf("Agent completed in %v", result.Duration)
    25→
    26→	// 2. Verify file was created with expected content
    27→	t.Log("Step 2: Verifying file was created")
    28→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"), "hello.go should exist")
    29→	AssertHelloWorldProgram(t, env, "hello.go")
    30→
    31→	// 3. Verify rewind points exist (session should have created checkpoints)
    32→	t.Log("Step 3: Checking for rewind points")
    33→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    34→	assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points), 1, "Should have at least 1 rewind point")
    35→	if len(points) > 0 {
    36→		t.Logf("Found %d rewind point(s), first: %s", len(points), points[0].Message)
    37→	}
    38→
    39→	// 4. User commits the changes with hooks
    40→	t.Log("Step 4: Committing changes with hooks")
    41→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add hello world program", "hello.go")
    42→
    43→	// 5. Verify checkpoint was created (trailer in commit)
    44→	t.Log("Step 5: Verifying checkpoint")
    45→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    46→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint in commit history")
    47→	assert.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID, "Commit should have Entire-Checkpoint trailer")
    48→	t.Logf("Checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
    49→
    50→	// 6. Verify metadata branch exists
    51→	t.Log("Step 6: Checking metadata branch")
    52→	assert.True(t, env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1"),
    53→		"entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist")
    54→}
    55→
    56→// TestE2E_MultipleChanges tests multiple agent changes before commit.
    57→func TestE2E_MultipleChanges(t *testing.T) {
    58→	t.Parallel()
    59→
    60→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    61→
    62→	// 1. First agent action: create hello.go
    63→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating first file")
    64→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    65→	require.NoError(t, err)
    66→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    67→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
    68→
    69→	// 2. Second agent action: create calc.go
    70→	t.Log("Step 2: Creating second file")
    71→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptCreateCalculator.Prompt)
    72→	require.NoError(t, err)
    73→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    74→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"))
    75→
    76→	// 3. Verify multiple rewind points exist
    77→	t.Log("Step 3: Checking rewind points")
    78→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    79→	assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points), 2, "Should have at least 2 rewind points")
    80→
    81→	// 4. Commit both files
    82→	t.Log("Step 4: Committing all changes")
    83→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add hello world and calculator", "hello.go", "calc.go")
    84→
    85→	// 5. Verify checkpoint
    86→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    87→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint in commit history")
    88→	assert.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID)
    89→}

    11→
    12→// TestE2E_CheckpointMetadata verifies that checkpoint metadata is correctly stored.
    13→func TestE2E_CheckpointMetadata(t *testing.T) {
    14→	t.Parallel()
    15→
    16→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    17→
    18→	// 1. Agent creates a file
    19→	t.Log("Step 1: Agent creating file")
    20→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateConfig.Prompt)
    21→	require.NoError(t, err)
    22→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    23→	AssertExpectedFilesExist(t, env, PromptCreateConfig)
    24→
    25→	// 2. Verify session created rewind points
    26→	t.Log("Step 2: Checking session rewind points")
    27→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    28→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points), 1, "Should have rewind points before commit")
    29→
    30→	// Note: Before commit, points are on the shadow branch
    31→	// They should have metadata directories set
    32→	for i, p := range points {
    33→		t.Logf("Rewind point %d: ID=%s, MetadataDir=%s, Message=%s",
    34→			i, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.MetadataDir, p.Message)
    35→	}
    36→
    37→	// 3. User commits
    38→	t.Log("Step 3: Committing changes")
    39→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add config file", "config.json")
    40→
    41→	// 4. Verify checkpoint trailer added
    42→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    43→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint ID in commit history")
    44→	require.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID, "Should have checkpoint ID in commit")
    45→	t.Logf("Checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
    46→
    47→	// 5. Verify metadata branch has content
    48→	assert.True(t, env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1"),
    49→		"Metadata branch should exist after commit")
    50→
    51→	// 6. Verify rewind points now reference condensed metadata
    52→	t.Log("Step 4: Checking post-commit rewind points")
    53→	postPoints := env.GetRewindPoints()
    54→	// After commit, logs-only points from entire/checkpoints/v1 should exist
    55→	for i, p := range postPoints {
    56→		t.Logf("Post-commit point %d: ID=%s, IsLogsOnly=%v, CondensationID=%s",
    57→			i, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.IsLogsOnly, p.CondensationID)
    58→	}
    59→}
    60→
    61→// TestE2E_CheckpointIDFormat verifies checkpoint ID format is correct.
    62→func TestE2E_CheckpointIDFormat(t *testing.T) {
    63→	t.Parallel()
    64→
    65→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    66→
    67→	// 1. Agent makes changes
    68→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    69→	require.NoError(t, err)
    70→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    71→
    72→	// 2. User commits
    73→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add hello world", "hello.go")
    74→
    75→	// 3. Verify checkpoint ID format
    76→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    77→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint ID in commit history")
    78→	require.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID)
    79→
    80→	// Checkpoint ID should be 12 hex characters
    81→	assert.Len(t, checkpointID, 12, "Checkpoint ID should be 12 characters")
    82→
    83→	// Should only contain hex characters
    84→	for _, c := range checkpointID {
    85→		assert.True(t, (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f'),
    86→			"Checkpoint ID should be lowercase hex: got %c", c)
    87→	}
    88→}
    89→
    90→// TestE2E_AutoCommitStrategy tests the auto-commit strategy creates clean commits.
    91→func TestE2E_AutoCommitStrategy(t *testing.T) {
    92→	t.Parallel()
    93→
    94→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "auto-commit")
    95→
    96→	// 1. Agent creates a file

   100→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
   101→
   102→	// 2. Verify file exists
   103→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
   104→
   105→	// 3. With auto-commit, commits are created automatically
   106→	// Check if commits were made with checkpoint trailers
   107→	commitMsg := env.GetCommitMessage(env.GetHeadHash())
   108→	t.Logf("Latest commit message: %s", commitMsg)
   109→
   110→	// 4. Verify metadata branch exists
   111→	if env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1") {
   112→		t.Log("Metadata branch exists (auto-commit creates it)")
   113→	}
   114→
   115→	// 5. Check for rewind points
   116→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
   117→	t.Logf("Found %d rewind points", len(points))
   118→}

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"testing"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
     9→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// TestE2E_RewindToCheckpoint tests rewinding to a previous checkpoint.
    13→func TestE2E_RewindToCheckpoint(t *testing.T) {
    14→	t.Parallel()
    15→
    16→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    17→
    18→	// 1. Agent creates first file
    19→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating first file")
    20→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    21→	require.NoError(t, err)
    22→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    23→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
    24→
    25→	// Get first checkpoint
    26→	points1 := env.GetRewindPoints()
    27→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points1), 1)
    28→	firstPointID := points1[0].ID
    29→	t.Logf("First checkpoint: %s", safeIDPrefix(firstPointID))
    30→
    31→	// Save original content
    32→	originalContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    33→
    34→	// 2. Agent modifies the file
    35→	t.Log("Step 2: Modifying file")
    36→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptModifyHelloGo.Prompt)
    37→	require.NoError(t, err)
    38→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    39→
    40→	// Verify content changed
    41→	modifiedContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    42→	assert.NotEqual(t, originalContent, modifiedContent, "Content should have changed")
    43→	assert.Contains(t, modifiedContent, "E2E Test", "Should contain new message")
    44→
    45→	// Get second checkpoint
    46→	points2 := env.GetRewindPoints()
    47→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points2), 2, "Should have at least 2 checkpoints")
    48→	t.Logf("Now have %d checkpoints", len(points2))
    49→
    50→	// 3. Rewind to first checkpoint
    51→	t.Log("Step 3: Rewinding to first checkpoint")
    52→	err = env.Rewind(firstPointID)
    53→	require.NoError(t, err)
    54→
    55→	// 4. Verify content was restored
    56→	t.Log("Step 4: Verifying content restored")
    57→	restoredContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    58→	assert.Equal(t, originalContent, restoredContent, "Content should be restored to original")
    59→	assert.NotContains(t, restoredContent, "E2E Test", "Should not contain modified message")
    60→}
    61→
    62→// TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit tests rewinding to a checkpoint after user commits.
    63→func TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {
    64→	t.Parallel()
    65→
    66→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    67→
    68→	// 1. Agent creates file
    69→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating file")
    70→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    71→	require.NoError(t, err)
    72→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    73→
    74→	// Get checkpoint before commit
    75→	pointsBefore := env.GetRewindPoints()
    76→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(pointsBefore), 1)
    77→	preCommitPointID := pointsBefore[0].ID
    78→
    79→	// 2. User commits
    80→	t.Log("Step 2: Committing")
    81→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add hello world", "hello.go")
    82→
    83→	// 3. Agent modifies file (new session)
    84→	t.Log("Step 3: Modifying file after commit")
    85→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptModifyHelloGo.Prompt)
    86→	require.NoError(t, err)
    87→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    88→
    89→	modifiedContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    90→	require.Contains(t, modifiedContent, "E2E Test")
    91→
    92→	// 4. Get rewind points - should include both pre and post commit points
    93→	t.Log("Step 4: Getting rewind points")
    94→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    95→	t.Logf("Found %d rewind points", len(points))
    96→	for i, p := range points {
    97→		t.Logf("  Point %d: %s (logs_only=%v, condensation_id=%s)",
    98→			i, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.IsLogsOnly, p.CondensationID)
    99→	}
   100→
   101→	// 5. Rewind to pre-commit checkpoint
   102→	// After commit, the pre-commit checkpoint becomes logs-only because the shadow branch
   103→	// is condensed. Rewinding to a logs-only point should fail with a clear error.
   104→	t.Log("Step 5: Attempting rewind to pre-commit (logs-only) checkpoint")
   105→	err = env.Rewind(preCommitPointID)
   106→
   107→	// Verify the current file state regardless of rewind result
   108→	currentContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
   109→
   110→	if err != nil {
   111→		// Rewind failed as expected for logs-only checkpoint
   112→		t.Logf("Rewind to logs-only point failed as expected: %v", err)
   113→		// File should still have the modifications since rewind failed
   114→		assert.Contains(t, currentContent, "E2E Test",
   115→			"File should still have modifications after logs-only rewind failure")
   116→	} else {
   117→		// If rewind succeeded, the file might have been restored
   118→		// This could happen if the rewind point wasn't actually logs-only
   119→		t.Log("Rewind succeeded - checkpoint may not have been logs-only")
   120→	}
   121→}
   122→
   123→// TestE2E_RewindMultipleFiles tests rewinding changes across multiple files.
   124→func TestE2E_RewindMultipleFiles(t *testing.T) {
   125→	t.Parallel()
   126→
   127→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
   128→
   129→	// 1. Agent creates multiple files
   130→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating first file")
   131→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
   132→	require.NoError(t, err)
   133→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
   134→
   135→	// Get checkpoint after first file
   136→	points1 := env.GetRewindPoints()
   137→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points1), 1)
   138→	afterFirstFile := points1[0].ID
   139→
   140→	t.Log("Step 2: Creating second file")
   141→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptCreateCalculator.Prompt)
   142→	require.NoError(t, err)
   143→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
   144→
   145→	// Verify both files exist
   146→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
   147→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"))
   148→
   149→	// 3. Rewind to after first file (before second)
   150→	t.Log("Step 3: Rewinding to after first file")
   151→	err = env.Rewind(afterFirstFile)
   152→	require.NoError(t, err)
   153→
   154→	// 4. Verify only first file exists
   155→	assert.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"), "hello.go should still exist")
   156→	assert.False(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"), "calc.go should be removed by rewind")
   157→}
   158→

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"testing"
     7→
     8→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
     9→)
    10→
    11→// TestE2E_AgentCommitsDuringTurn tests what happens when the agent commits during its turn.
    12→// This is a P1 test because it tests the deferred finalization behavior.
    13→func TestE2E_AgentCommitsDuringTurn(t *testing.T) {
    14→	t.Parallel()
    15→
    16→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    17→
    18→	// 1. First, agent creates a file
    19→	t.Log("Step 1: Agent creating file")
    20→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    21→	require.NoError(t, err)
    22→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    23→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
    24→
    25→	// 2. Agent commits the changes (using Bash tool)
    26→	t.Log("Step 2: Agent committing changes")
    27→	commitPrompt := `Stage and commit the hello.go file with commit message "Add hello world via agent".
    28→Use these exact commands:
    29→1. git add hello.go
    30→2. git commit -m "Add hello world via agent"
    31→Only run these two commands, nothing else.`
    32→
    33→	result, err = env.RunAgentWithTools(commitPrompt, []string{"Bash"})
    34→	require.NoError(t, err)
    35→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    36→	t.Logf("Agent commit output: %s", result.Stdout)
    37→
    38→	// 3. Verify the commit was made
    39→	t.Log("Step 3: Verifying commit was made")
    40→	headMsg := env.GetCommitMessage(env.GetHeadHash())
    41→	t.Logf("HEAD commit message: %s", headMsg)
    42→
    43→	// The commit might or might not have the Entire-Checkpoint trailer depending
    44→	// on hook configuration. The key thing is the commit was made.
    45→
    46→	// 4. Check rewind points
    47→	t.Log("Step 4: Checking rewind points")
    48→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    49→	t.Logf("Found %d rewind points after agent commit", len(points))
    50→
    51→	// 5. Agent makes another change after committing
    52→	t.Log("Step 5: Agent making another change")
    53→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptCreateCalculator.Prompt)
    54→	require.NoError(t, err)
    55→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    56→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"))
    57→
    58→	// 6. User commits the second change
    59→	t.Log("Step 6: User committing second change")
    60→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add calculator", "calc.go")
    61→
    62→	// 7. Final verification
    63→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    64→	if err != nil {
    65→		t.Logf("No checkpoint ID found: %v", err)
    66→	} else {
    67→		t.Logf("Final checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
    68→	}
    69→}
    70→
    71→// TestE2E_MultipleAgentSessions tests behavior across multiple agent sessions.
    72→func TestE2E_MultipleAgentSessions(t *testing.T) {
    73→	t.Parallel()
    74→
    75→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    76→
    77→	// Session 1: Create hello.go
    78→	t.Log("Session 1: Creating hello.go")
    79→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    80→	require.NoError(t, err)
    81→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    82→
    83→	session1Points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    84→	t.Logf("After session 1: %d rewind points", len(session1Points))
    85→
    86→	// User commits
    87→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Session 1: Add hello world", "hello.go")
    88→
    89→	// Session 2: Create calc.go
    90→	t.Log("Session 2: Creating calc.go")
    91→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptCreateCalculator.Prompt)
    92→	require.NoError(t, err)
    93→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    94→
    95→	session2Points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    96→	t.Logf("After session 2: %d rewind points", len(session2Points))
    97→
    98→	// User commits
    99→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Session 2: Add calculator", "calc.go")
   100→
   101→	// Session 3: Add multiply function
   102→	t.Log("Session 3: Adding multiply function")
   103→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptAddMultiplyFunction.Prompt)
   104→	require.NoError(t, err)
   105→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
   106→
   107→	// Verify multiply function was added
   108→	AssertCalculatorFunctions(t, env, "calc.go", "Add", "Subtract", "Multiply")
   109→
   110→	// User commits
   111→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Session 3: Add multiply function", "calc.go")
   112→
   113→	// Final check: we should have checkpoint IDs in commit history
   114→	t.Log("Final verification")
   115→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
   116→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint in commit history")
   117→	require.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID, "Should have checkpoint in final commit")
   118→}
   119→

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"testing"
     8→
     9→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/assert"
    10→	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// These tests cover the scenarios documented in docs/architecture/checkpoint-scenarios.md
    14→
    15→// TestE2E_Scenario3_MultipleGranularCommits tests Claude making multiple granular commits
    16→// during a single turn.
    17→//
    18→// Scenario 3: Claude Makes Multiple Granular Commits
    19→// - Claude is instructed to make granular commits
    20→// - Multiple commits happen during one turn
    21→// - Each commit gets its own unique checkpoint ID
    22→// - All checkpoints are finalized together at turn end
    23→func TestE2E_Scenario3_MultipleGranularCommits(t *testing.T) {
    24→	t.Parallel()
    25→
    26→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    27→
    28→	// Count commits before
    29→	commitsBefore := env.GetCommitCount()
    30→	t.Logf("Commits before: %d", commitsBefore)
    31→
    32→	// Agent creates multiple files and commits them separately
    33→	granularCommitPrompt := `Please do the following tasks, committing after each one:
    34→
    35→1. Create a file called file1.go with this content:
    36→   package main
    37→   func One() int { return 1 }
    38→   Then run: git add file1.go && git commit -m "Add file1"
    39→
    40→2. Create a file called file2.go with this content:
    41→   package main
    42→   func Two() int { return 2 }
    43→   Then run: git add file2.go && git commit -m "Add file2"
    44→
    45→3. Create a file called file3.go with this content:
    46→   package main
    47→   func Three() int { return 3 }
    48→   Then run: git add file3.go && git commit -m "Add file3"
    49→
    50→Do each task in order, making the commit after each file creation.`
    51→
    52→	result, err := env.RunAgentWithTools(granularCommitPrompt, []string{"Write", "Bash"})
    53→	require.NoError(t, err)
    54→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    55→	t.Logf("Agent output: %s", result.Stdout)
    56→
    57→	// Verify all files were created
    58→	assert.True(t, env.FileExists("file1.go"), "file1.go should exist")
    59→	assert.True(t, env.FileExists("file2.go"), "file2.go should exist")
    60→	assert.True(t, env.FileExists("file3.go"), "file3.go should exist")
    61→
    62→	// Verify multiple commits were made
    63→	commitsAfter := env.GetCommitCount()
    64→	t.Logf("Commits after: %d", commitsAfter)
    65→	assert.GreaterOrEqual(t, commitsAfter-commitsBefore, 3, "Should have at least 3 new commits")
    66→
    67→	// Get all checkpoint IDs from history
    68→	checkpointIDs := env.GetAllCheckpointIDsFromHistory()
    69→	t.Logf("Found %d checkpoint IDs in commit history", len(checkpointIDs))
    70→	for i, id := range checkpointIDs {
    71→		t.Logf("  Checkpoint %d: %s", i, id)
    72→	}
    73→
    74→	// Each commit should have its own unique checkpoint ID (1:1 model)
    75→	if len(checkpointIDs) >= 3 {
    76→		// Verify checkpoint IDs are unique
    77→		idSet := make(map[string]bool)
    78→		for _, id := range checkpointIDs {
    79→			require.Falsef(t, idSet[id], "Checkpoint IDs should be unique: %s is duplicated", id)
    80→			idSet[id] = true
    81→		}
    82→	}
    83→
    84→	// Verify metadata branch exists
    85→	assert.True(t, env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1"),
    86→		"entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist")
    87→
    88→	// Validate each checkpoint has proper metadata and content
    89→	// Note: checkpointIDs are in reverse chronological order (newest first)
    90→	// So checkpointIDs[0] = file3.go, [1] = file2.go, [2] = file1.go
    91→	//
    92→	// With deferred finalization, all checkpoints from the same turn get the
    93→	// FULL transcript at turn end, so all checkpoints should contain all file names.
    94→	allFiles := []string{"file1.go", "file2.go", "file3.go"}
    95→	for i, cpID := range checkpointIDs {
    96→		fileNum := len(checkpointIDs) - i // Reverse the index to match file numbers
    97→		fileName := fmt.Sprintf("file%d.go", fileNum)
    98→		t.Logf("Validating checkpoint %d: %s (files_touched: %s)", i, cpID, fileName)
    99→		env.ValidateCheckpoint(CheckpointValidation{
   100→			CheckpointID:              cpID,
   101→			Strategy:                  "manual-commit",
   102→			FilesTouched:              []string{fileName},
   103→			ExpectedTranscriptContent: allFiles, // All checkpoints have full transcript
   104→		})
   105→	}
   106→}
