```
     1→# Sessions and Checkpoints
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→Entire CLI creates checkpoints for AI coding sessions. The system is agent-agnostic - it works with Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot, or any tool that triggers Entire hooks.
     6→
     7→## Domain Model
     8→
     9→### Session
    10→
    11→A **Session** is a unit of work. Defined in `strategy/session.go`:
    12→
    13→```go
    14→type Session struct {
    15→    ID          string       // e.g., "2025-12-01-8f76b0e8-b8f1-4a87-9186-848bdd83d62e"
    16→    Description string       // Human-readable summary (first prompt or derived)
    17→    Strategy    string       // Strategy that created this session
    18→    StartTime   time.Time
    19→    Checkpoints []Checkpoint
    20→}
    21→```
    22→
    23→### Checkpoint
    24→
    25→A **Checkpoint** captures a point-in-time within a session. Defined in `strategy/session.go`:
    26→
    27→```go
    28→type Checkpoint struct {
    29→    CheckpointID     id.CheckpointID // Stable 12-hex-char identifier
    30→    Message          string          // Commit message or checkpoint description
    31→    Timestamp        time.Time
    32→    IsTaskCheckpoint bool            // Task checkpoint (subagent) vs session checkpoint
    33→    ToolUseID        string          // Tool use ID for task checkpoints (empty for session)
    34→}
    35→```
    36→
    37→### Checkpoint Types
    38→
    39→The low-level `checkpoint.Type` (from `checkpoint/checkpoint.go`) indicates storage location:
    40→
    41→```go
    42→type Type int
    43→
    44→const (
    45→    Temporary Type = iota // Full state snapshot, shadow branch
    46→    Committed             // Metadata + commit ref, entire/checkpoints/v1
    47→)
    48→```
    49→
    50→| Type | Contents | Use Case |
    51→|------|----------|----------|
    52→| Temporary | Full state (code + metadata) | Intra-session rewind, pre-commit |
    53→| Committed | Metadata + commit reference | Permanent record, post-commit rewind |
    54→
    55→## Interface
    56→
    57→### Session Operations
    58→
    59→Sessions are accessed via standalone functions in `strategy/session.go`:
    60→
    61→```go
    62→// ListSessions returns all sessions from entire/checkpoints/v1,
    63→// plus additional sessions from strategies implementing SessionSource.
    64→func ListSessions() ([]Session, error)
    65→
    66→// GetSession finds a session by ID (supports prefix matching).
    67→func GetSession(sessionID string) (*Session, error)
    68→```
    69→
    70→### Checkpoint Storage (Low-Level)
    71→
    72→The `checkpoint.Store` interface (from `checkpoint/checkpoint.go`) provides primitives for reading/writing checkpoints. Used by strategies.
    73→
    74→```go
    75→type Store interface {
    76→    // Temporary checkpoint operations (shadow branches - full state)
    77→    WriteTemporary(ctx context.Context, opts WriteTemporaryOptions) (WriteTemporaryResult, error)
    78→    ReadTemporary(ctx context.Context, baseCommit, worktreeID string) (*ReadTemporaryResult, error)
    79→    ListTemporary(ctx context.Context) ([]TemporaryInfo, error)
    80→
    81→    // Committed checkpoint operations (entire/checkpoints/v1 branch - metadata only)
    82→    WriteCommitted(ctx context.Context, opts WriteCommittedOptions) error
    83→    ReadCommitted(ctx context.Context, checkpointID id.CheckpointID) (*CheckpointSummary, error)
    84→    ReadSessionContent(ctx context.Context, checkpointID id.CheckpointID, sessionIndex int) (*SessionContent, error)
    85→    ReadSessionContentByID(ctx context.Context, checkpointID id.CheckpointID, sessionID string) (*SessionContent, error)
    86→    ListCommitted(ctx context.Context) ([]CommittedInfo, error)
    87→}
    88→```
    89→
    90→Key option types (abbreviated):
    91→
    92→```go
    93→type WriteTemporaryOptions struct {
    94→    SessionID      string
    95→    BaseCommit     string
    96→    WorktreeID     string   // Internal git worktree identifier (empty for main)
    97→    ModifiedFiles  []string
    98→    NewFiles       []string
    99→    DeletedFiles   []string
   100→    MetadataDir    string   // Relative path to metadata directory
   101→    MetadataDirAbs string   // Absolute path
   102→    CommitMessage  string
   103→    // ...
   104→}
   105→
   106→type WriteCommittedOptions struct {
   107→    CheckpointID id.CheckpointID
   108→    SessionID    string
   109→    Strategy     string
   110→    Branch       string
   111→    Transcript   []byte
   112→    Prompts      []string
   113→    Context      []byte
   114→    FilesTouched []string
   115→    TokenUsage   *agent.TokenUsage
   116→    // ...
   117→}
   118→```
   119→
   120→Token usage is defined in `agent/types.go`:
   121→
   122→```go
   123→type TokenUsage struct {
   124→    InputTokens         int         `json:"input_tokens"`
   125→    CacheCreationTokens int         `json:"cache_creation_tokens"`
   126→    CacheReadTokens     int         `json:"cache_read_tokens"`
   127→    OutputTokens        int         `json:"output_tokens"`
   128→    APICallCount        int         `json:"api_call_count"`
   129→    SubagentTokens      *TokenUsage `json:"subagent_tokens,omitempty"`
   130→}
   131→```
   132→
   133→### Strategy-Level Operations
   134→
   135→Strategies compose low-level primitives into higher-level workflows.
   136→
   137→**Manual-commit** has condensation logic:
   138→
   139→```go
   140→// CondenseSession reads accumulated temporary state and writes a committed checkpoint.
   141→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) CondenseSession(
   142→    repo *git.Repository,
   143→    checkpointID id.CheckpointID,
   144→    state *SessionState,
   145→) (*CondenseResult, error)
   146→```
   147→
   148→**Auto-commit** writes committed checkpoints directly:
   149→
   150→```go
   151→// SaveChanges creates a commit on the active branch and writes metadata.
   152→func (s *AutoCommitStrategy) SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext) error
   153→```
   154→
   155→## Storage
   156→
   157→| Type | Location | Contents |
   158→|------|----------|----------|
   159→| Session State | `.git/entire-sessions/<id>.json` | Active session tracking |
   160→| Temporary | `entire/<commit[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>` branch | Full state (code + metadata) |
   161→| Committed | `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch (sharded) | Metadata + commit reference |
   162→
   163→### Session State
   164→
   165→Location: `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`
   166→
   167→Stored in git common dir (shared across worktrees). Tracks active session info.
   168→
   169→### Temporary Checkpoints
   170→
   171→Branch: `entire/<commit[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>`
   172→
   173→Contains full worktree snapshot plus metadata overlay. **Multiple concurrent sessions** can share the same shadow branch - their checkpoints interleave:
   174→
   175→```
   176→<worktree files...>
   177→.entire/metadata/<session-id-1>/
   178→├── full.jsonl           # Session 1 transcript
   179→├── prompt.txt           # User prompts
   180→├── context.md           # Generated context
   181→└── tasks/<tool-use-id>/ # Task checkpoints
   182→.entire/metadata/<session-id-2>/
   183→├── full.jsonl           # Session 2 transcript (concurrent)
   184→├── ...
   185→```
   186→
   187→Tied to a base commit. Condensed to committed on user commit.
   188→
   189→**Shadow branch lifecycle:**
   190→- Created on first checkpoint for a base commit
   191→- Migrated automatically if base commit changes (stash → pull → apply scenario)
   192→- Deleted after condensation to `entire/checkpoints/v1`
   193→- Reset if orphaned (no session state file exists)
   194→
   195→### Committed Checkpoints
   196→
   197→Branch: `entire/checkpoints/v1`
   198→
   199→Metadata only, sharded by checkpoint ID. Supports **multiple sessions per checkpoint**:
   200→
   201→```
   202→<id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/
   203→├── metadata.json        # CheckpointSummary (aggregated stats)
   204→├── 0/                   # First session (0-based indexing)
   205→│   ├── metadata.json    # Session-specific CommittedMetadata
   206→│   ├── full.jsonl
   207→│   ├── prompt.txt
   208→│   ├── context.md
   209→│   └── content_hash.txt
   210→├── 1/                   # Second session
   211→│   ├── metadata.json
   212→│   ├── full.jsonl
   213→│   └── ...
   214→└── 2/                   # Third session...
   215→```
   216→
   217→**Root-level metadata.json (`CheckpointSummary`):**
   218→```json
   219→{
   220→  "checkpoint_id": "abc123def456",
   221→  "strategy": "manual-commit",
   222→  "branch": "main",
   223→  "checkpoints_count": 3,
   224→  "files_touched": ["file1.txt", "file2.txt"],
   225→  "sessions": [
   226→    {
   227→      "metadata": "/ab/c123def456/0/metadata.json",
   228→      "transcript": "/ab/c123def456/0/full.jsonl",
   229→      "context": "/ab/c123def456/0/context.md",
   230→      "content_hash": "/ab/c123def456/0/content_hash.txt",
   231→      "prompt": "/ab/c123def456/0/prompt.txt"
   232→    }
   233→  ],
   234→  "token_usage": {
   235→    "input_tokens": 1500,
   236→    "cache_creation_tokens": 200,
   237→    "cache_read_tokens": 800,
   238→    "output_tokens": 500,
   239→    "api_call_count": 3
   240→  }
   241→}
   242→```
   243→
   244→When condensing multiple concurrent sessions:
   245→- All sessions are stored in numbered subdirectories using 0-based indexing (`0/`, `1/`, `2/`, ...)
   246→- Each `session_id` is assigned a stable index; subsequent writes for the same session reuse the same numbered folder
   247→- New `session_id` values are appended at the next index, so higher-numbered folders correspond to more recently introduced sessions, not necessarily the chronologically latest activity
   248→- `sessions` array in `CheckpointSummary` maps each session to its file paths
   249→- `files_touched` is merged from all sessions
   250→
   251→### Checkpoint ID Linking
   252→
   253→The checkpoint ID is the **stable identifier** that links user commits to metadata across branches.
   254→
   255→**Format:** 12-hex-character random ID (e.g., `a3b2c4d5e6f7`)
   256→
   257→**Generation:**
   258→- Manual-commit: Generated during condensation (post-commit hook)
   259→- Auto-commit: Generated when creating the commit
   260→
   261→**Usage:**
   262→
   263→1. **User commit trailer** (both strategies):
   264→   - `Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7` added to user's commit message
   265→   - Auto-commit: Added programmatically
   266→   - Manual-commit: Added by `prepare-commit-msg` hook (user can remove)
   267→
   268→2. **Directory sharding** on `entire/checkpoints/v1`:
   269→   - Path: `<id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/` (e.g., `a3/b2c4d5e6f7/`)
   270→   - First 2 chars = shard (256 possible shards)
   271→   - Remaining 10 chars = directory name
   272→
   273→3. **Commit subject** on `entire/checkpoints/v1`:
   274→   - Format: `Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7`
   275→   - Makes `git log entire/checkpoints/v1` readable
   276→
   277→**Bidirectional Lookup:**
   278→
   279→```
   280→User commit → Metadata:
   281→  1. Extract "Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7" from commit message
   282→  2. Read entire/checkpoints/v1 tree at a3/b2c4d5e6f7/
   283→
   284→Metadata → User commits:
   285→  Given checkpoint ID a3b2c4d5e6f7
   286→  → Search branch history for commits with "Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7"
   287→```
   288→
   289→Note: Commit subjects on `entire/checkpoints/v1` (e.g., `Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7`)
   290→are for human readability in `git log` only. The CLI always reads from the tree at HEAD.
   291→
   292→**Example Flow:**
   293→
   294→```
   295→                    User creates commit
   296→                           ↓
   297→           prepare-commit-msg hook adds trailer
   298→                           ↓
   299→┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   300→│ Commit on main branch:                           │
   301→│   "Implement login feature                       │
   302→│                                                   │
   303→│   Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7"               │
   304→└──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   305→                           ↓
   306→                  post-commit hook runs
   307→                           ↓
   308→          Condense shadow → entire/checkpoints/v1
   309→                           ↓
   310→┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   311→│ Commit on entire/checkpoints/v1:                 │
   312→│   Subject: "Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7"            │
   313→│                                                   │
   314→│   Tree: a3/b2c4d5e6f7/                           │
   315→│     ├── metadata.json                            │
   316→│     │   (checkpoint_id: "a3b2c4d5e6f7")          │
   317→│     ├── 0/                                       │
   318→│     │   ├── full.jsonl                           │
   319→│     │   ├── prompt.txt                           │
   320→│     │   └── context.md                           │
   321→│     └── ...                                      │
   322→│                                                   │
   323→│   Trailers:                                      │
   324→│     Entire-Session: 2026-01-20-uuid              │
   325→│     Entire-Strategy: manual-commit               │
   326→└──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   327→```
   328→
   329→The checkpoint ID creates a **bidirectional link**: user commits can find their metadata, and metadata can find the commits that reference it.
   330→
   331→### Package Structure
   332→
   333→```
   334→strategy/
   335→├── session.go           # Session and Checkpoint types, ListSessions(), GetSession()
   336→
   337→session/
   338→├── state.go             # Active session state (StateStore, .git/entire-sessions/)
   339→├── phase.go             # Session phase state machine (ACTIVE, IDLE, ENDED, etc.)
   340→
   341→checkpoint/
   342→├── checkpoint.go        # checkpoint.Type, checkpoint.Store interface, CheckpointSummary, etc.
   343→├── store.go             # GitStore implementation
   344→├── temporary.go         # Shadow branch storage
   345→├── committed.go         # Metadata branch storage
   346→├── id/                  # CheckpointID type and generation
   347→│   └── id.go
   348→```
   349→
   350→Strategies use `checkpoint.Store` primitives - storage details are encapsulated.
   351→
   352→## Strategy Role
   353→
   354→Strategies determine checkpoint timing and type:
   355→
   356→| Strategy | On Save | On Task Complete | On User Commit |
   357→|----------|---------|------------------|----------------|
   358→| Manual-commit | Temporary | Temporary | Condense → Committed |
   359→| Auto-commit | Committed | Committed | — |
   360→
   361→## Rewind
   362→
   363→Each `RewindPoint` includes `SessionID` and `SessionPrompt` to help identify which checkpoint belongs to which session when multiple sessions are interleaved.
   364→
   365→## Concurrent Sessions
   366→
   367→Multiple AI sessions can run concurrently on the same base commit:
   368→
   369→1. **Warning on start** - When a second session starts while another has uncommitted checkpoints, a warning is shown
   370→2. **Both proceed** - User can continue; checkpoints interleave on the same shadow branch
   371→3. **Identification** - Each checkpoint is tagged with its session ID; rewind UI shows session prompt
   372→4. **Condensation** - On commit, all sessions are condensed together with archived subfolders
   373→
   374→### Conflict Handling
   375→
   376→| Scenario | Behavior |
   377→|----------|----------|
   378→| Concurrent sessions (same worktree) | Warning shown, both proceed |
   379→| Orphaned shadow branch (no state file) | Branch reset, new session proceeds |
   380→| Cross-worktree conflict (state file exists) | `SessionIDConflictError` returned |
   381→
   382→### Shadow Branch Migration
   383→
   384→If user does stash → pull → apply (HEAD changes without commit):
   385→- Detection: base commit changed AND old shadow branch still exists
   386→- Action: branch renamed from `entire/<old-commit[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>` to `entire/<new-commit[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>`
   387→- Result: session continues with checkpoints preserved
   388→
   389→---
   390→
   391→## Appendix: Legacy Names
   392→
   393→| Current | Legacy |
   394→|---------|--------|
   395→| Manual-commit | Shadow |
   396→| Auto-commit | Dual |
   397→

Now I have a complete picture. Here's my review of the e2e integration tests:

## E2E Test Review

### Overview

The e2e tests are in `cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/` and use the `//go:build e2e` tag. They're well-structured and run actual agent prompts against a real Claude Code installation.

### Alignment with Documentation ✓

**Strong alignment with `checkpoint-scenarios.md`:**
- `TestE2E_Scenario1_BasicFlow` - Scenario 1: Basic prompt → commit flow
- `TestE2E_Scenario2_AgentCommitsDuringTurn` - Scenario 2: Agent commits during turn
- `TestE2E_Scenario3_MultipleGranularCommits` - Scenario 3: Multiple granular commits
- `TestE2E_Scenario4_UserSplitsCommits` - Scenario 4: User splits commits
- `TestE2E_Scenario5_PartialCommitStashNextPrompt` - Scenario 5: Partial commit + stash
- `TestE2E_Scenario6_StashSecondPromptUnstashCommitAll` - Scenario 6: Stash + unstash + commit all
- `TestE2E_Scenario7_PartialStagingSimulated` - Scenario 7: Partial staging with `git add -p`
- `TestE2E_ContentAwareOverlap_RevertAndReplace` - Content-aware overlap detection

### Issues Found

**1. CLAUDE.md doesn't document e2e tests**

The CLAUDE.md mentions:
- `mise run test` - unit tests
- `mise run test:integration` - integration tests at `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/`
- `mise run test:ci` - all tests

But there's no mention of e2e tests or how to run them. Users wouldn't know about the `e2e` build tag.

**2. Weak auto-commit strategy coverage**

`TestE2E_AutoCommitStrategy` (scenario_checkpoint_test.go:91-118) doesn't use `ValidateCheckpoint` and has weaker assertions:
```go
// Just logs, doesn't assert:
if env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1") {
    t.Log("Metadata branch exists (auto-commit creates it)")
}
```

Compare to manual-commit tests that call `env.ValidateCheckpoint()`.

**3. Non-deterministic rewind test**

`TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit` accepts both success and failure as valid outcomes:
```go
if err != nil {
    t.Logf("Rewind to logs-only point failed as expected: %v", err)
} else {
    t.Log("Rewind succeeded - checkpoint may not have been logs-only")
}
```

This makes the test pass regardless of actual behavior.

**4. Missing scenarios from documentation**

Not tested in e2e (though some are in integration tests):
- Shadow branch migration (stash → pull → apply)
- Concurrent session warning
- Cross-worktree scenarios
- Pre-push hook behavior

**5. Gemini CLI runner stub**

`GeminiCLIRunner.RunPromptWithTools` returns `errors.New("gemini CLI runner not yet implemented")`. This is fine but should be documented.

**6. Unused `safeIDPrefix` helper**

Defined in `testenv.go:539-544` but only used for logging, not actual assertions.

### What's Working Well

1. **Agent-agnostic design** - `E2E_AGENT` env var, `AgentRunner` interface
2. **Proper test isolation** - Each test gets its own temp repo via `NewFeatureBranchEnv`
3. **Comprehensive `ValidateCheckpoint`** - Verifies metadata structure, JSONL validity, content hashes
4. **Deterministic prompts** - `prompts.go` uses explicit requirements to reduce agent variance
5. **Parallel execution** - All tests call `t.Parallel()`
6. **Build tag separation** - `//go:build e2e` keeps these from regular test runs
7. **Good hook testing** - `GitCommitWithShadowHooks` manually invokes prepare-commit-msg and post-commit
8. **Modifying existing files** - Tests cover both new file creation and modifying tracked files

### Recommendations

1. **Update CLAUDE.md** to document e2e tests:
   ```markdown
   ### Running E2E Tests
   ```bash
   E2E_AGENT=claude-code go test -tags=e2e ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...
   ```
   E2E tests require Claude Code to be installed and authenticated.
   ```

2. **Strengthen auto-commit tests** - Add `ValidateCheckpoint` calls

3. **Make logs-only rewind test deterministic** - Assert specific expected behavior

4. **Consider adding worktree e2e test** if this is a critical path

The tests are generally well-designed and align with documented scenarios. The main gap is documentation about how to run them and some weaker assertions in a few tests.

     1→# Entire - CLI 
     2→
     3→This repo contains the CLI for Entire.
     4→
     5→## Architecture
     6→
     7→- CLI build with github.com/spf13/cobra and github.com/charmbracelet/huh 
     8→
     9→## Key Directories
    10→
    11→### Commands (`cmd/`)
    12→- `entire/`: Main CLI entry point
    13→- `entire/cli`: CLI utilities and helpers
    14→- `entire/cli/commands`: actual command implementations
    15→- `entire/cli/strategy`: strategy implementations - see section below
    16→- `entire/cli/checkpoint`: checkpoint storage abstractions (temporary and committed)
    17→- `entire/cli/session`: session state management
    18→- `entire/cli/integration_test`: integration tests
    19→
    20→## Tech Stack
    21→
    22→- Language: Go 1.25.x
    23→- Build tool: mise, go modules
    24→- Linting: golangci-lint
    25→
    26→## Development
    27→
    28→### Running Tests
    29→```bash
    30→mise run test
    31→```
    32→
    33→### Running Integration Tests
    34→```bash
    35→mise run test:integration
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Running All Tests (CI)
    39→```bash
    40→mise run test:ci
    41→```
    42→
    43→Integration tests use the `//go:build integration` build tag and are located in `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/`.
    44→
    45→### Test Parallelization
    46→
    47→**Always use `t.Parallel()` in tests.** Every top-level test function and subtest should call `t.Parallel()` unless it modifies process-global state (e.g., `os.Chdir()`).
    48→
    49→```go
    50→func TestFeature_Foo(t *testing.T) {
    51→    t.Parallel()
    52→    // ...
    53→}
    54→
    55→// Integration tests: RunForAllStrategies handles t.Parallel() for subtests internally,
    56→// but the top-level test still needs it
    57→func TestFeature_Bar(t *testing.T) {
    58→    t.Parallel()
    59→    RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
    60→        // ...
    61→    })
    62→}
    63→```
    64→
    65→**Exception:** Tests that modify process-global state cannot be parallelized. This includes `os.Chdir()`/`t.Chdir()` and `os.Setenv()`/`t.Setenv()` — Go's test framework will panic if these are used after `t.Parallel()`.
    66→
    67→### Linting and Formatting
    68→```bash
    69→mise run fmt && mise run lint
    70→```
    71→
    72→### Before Every Commit (REQUIRED)
    73→
    74→**CI will fail if you skip these steps:**
    75→
    76→```bash
    77→mise run fmt      # Format code (CI enforces gofmt)
    78→mise run lint     # Lint check (CI enforces golangci-lint)
    79→mise run test:ci  # Run all tests (unit + integration)
    80→```
    81→
    82→Or combined: `mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci`
    83→
    84→**Common CI failures from skipping this:**
    85→- `gofmt` formatting differences → run `mise run fmt`
    86→- Lint errors → run `mise run lint` and fix issues
    87→- Test failures → run `mise run test` and fix
    88→
    89→### Code Duplication Prevention
    90→
    91→Before implementing Go code, use `/go:discover-related` to find existing utilities and patterns that might be reusable.
    92→
    93→**Check for duplication:**
    94→```bash
    95→mise run dup           # Comprehensive check (threshold 50) with summary
    96→mise run dup:staged    # Check only staged files
    97→mise run lint          # Normal lint includes dupl at threshold 75 (new issues only)
    98→mise run lint:full     # All issues at threshold 75
    99→```
   100→
   101→**Tiered thresholds:**
   102→- **75 tokens** (lint/CI) - Blocks on serious duplication (~20+ lines)
   103→- **50 tokens** (dup) - Advisory, catches smaller patterns (~10+ lines)
   104→
   105→When duplication is found:
   106→1. Check if a helper already exists in `common.go` or nearby utility files
   107→2. If not, consider extracting the duplicated logic to a shared helper
   108→3. If duplication is intentional (e.g., test setup), add a `//nolint:dupl` comment with explanation
   109→
   110→## Code Patterns
   111→
   112→### Error Handling
   113→
   114→The CLI uses a specific pattern for error output to avoid duplication between Cobra and main.go.
   115→
   116→**How it works:**
   117→- `root.go` sets `SilenceErrors: true` globally - Cobra never prints errors
   118→- `main.go` prints errors to stderr, unless the error is a `SilentError`
   119→- Commands return `NewSilentError(err)` when they've already printed a custom message
   120→
   121→**When to use `SilentError`:**
   122→Use `NewSilentError()` when you want to print a custom, user-friendly error message instead of the raw error:
   123→
   124→```go
   125→// In a command's RunE function:
   126→if _, err := paths.RepoRoot(); err != nil {
   127→    cmd.SilenceUsage = true  // Don't show usage for prerequisite errors
   128→    fmt.Fprintln(cmd.ErrOrStderr(), "Not a git repository. Please run 'entire enable' from within a git repository.")
   129→    return NewSilentError(errors.New("not a git repository"))
   130→}
   131→```
   132→
   133→**When NOT to use `SilentError`:**
   134→For normal errors where the default error message is sufficient, just return the error directly. main.go will print it:
   135→
   136→```go
   137→// Normal error - main.go will print "unknown strategy: foo"
   138→return fmt.Errorf("unknown strategy: %s", name)
   139→```
   140→
   141→**Key files:**
   142→- `errors.go` - Defines `SilentError` type and `NewSilentError()` constructor
   143→- `root.go` - Sets `SilenceErrors: true` on root command
   144→- `main.go` - Checks for `SilentError` before printing
   145→
   146→### Settings
   147→
   148→All settings access should go through the `settings` package (`cmd/entire/cli/settings/`).
   149→
   150→**Why a separate package:**
   151→The `settings` package exists to avoid import cycles. The `cli` package imports `strategy`, so `strategy` cannot import `cli`. The `settings` package provides shared settings loading that both can use.
   152→
   153→**Usage:**
   154→```go
   155→import "github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/settings"
   156→
   157→// Load full settings object
   158→s, err := settings.Load()
   159→if err != nil {
   160→    // handle error
   161→}
   162→if s.Enabled {
   163→    // ...
   164→}
   165→
   166→// Or use convenience functions
   167→if settings.IsSummarizeEnabled() {
   168→    // ...
   169→}
   170→```
   171→
   172→**Do NOT:**
   173→- Read `.entire/settings.json` or `.entire/settings.local.json` directly with `os.ReadFile`
   174→- Duplicate settings parsing logic in other packages
   175→- Create new settings helpers without adding them to the `settings` package
   176→
   177→**Key files:**
   178→- `settings/settings.go` - `EntireSettings` struct, `Load()`, and helper methods
   179→- `config.go` - Higher-level config functions that use settings (for `cli` package consumers)
   180→
   181→### Logging vs User Output
   182→
   183→- **Internal/debug logging**: Use `logging.Debug/Info/Warn/Error(ctx, msg, attrs...)` from `cmd/entire/cli/logging/`. Writes to `.entire/logs/`.
   184→- **User-facing output**: Use `fmt.Fprint*(cmd.OutOrStdout(), ...)` or `cmd.ErrOrStderr()`.
   185→
   186→Don't use `fmt.Print*` for operational messages (checkpoint saves, hook invocations, strategy decisions) - those should use the `logging` package.
   187→
   188→**Privacy**: Don't log user content (prompts, file contents, commit messages). Log only operational metadata (IDs, counts, paths, durations).
   189→
   190→### Git Operations
   191→
   192→We use github.com/go-git/go-git for most git operations, but with important exceptions:
   193→
   194→#### go-git v5 Bugs - Use CLI Instead
   195→
   196→**Do NOT use go-git v5 for `checkout` or `reset --hard` operations.**
   197→
   198→go-git v5 has a bug where `worktree.Reset()` with `git.HardReset` and `worktree.Checkout()` incorrectly delete untracked directories even when they're listed in `.gitignore`. This would destroy `.entire/` and `.worktrees/` directories.
   199→
   200→Use the git CLI instead:
   201→```go
   202→// WRONG - go-git deletes ignored directories
   203→worktree.Reset(&git.ResetOptions{
   204→    Commit: hash,
   205→    Mode:   git.HardReset,
   206→})
   207→
   208→// CORRECT - use git CLI
   209→cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "reset", "--hard", hash.String())
   210→```
   211→
   212→See `HardResetWithProtection()` in `common.go` and `CheckoutBranch()` in `git_operations.go` for examples.
   213→
   214→Regression tests in `hard_reset_test.go` verify this behavior - if go-git v6 fixes this issue, those tests can be used to validate switching back.
   215→
   216→#### Repo Root vs Current Working Directory
   217→
   218→**Always use repo root (not `os.Getwd()`) when working with git-relative paths.**
   219→
   220→Git commands like `git status` and `worktree.Status()` return paths relative to the **repository root**, not the current working directory. When Claude runs from a subdirectory (e.g., `/repo/frontend`), using `os.Getwd()` to construct absolute paths will produce incorrect results for files in sibling directories.
   221→
   222→```go
   223→// WRONG - breaks when running from subdirectory
   224→cwd, _ := os.Getwd()  // e.g., /repo/frontend
   225→absPath := filepath.Join(cwd, file)  // file="api/src/types.ts" → /repo/frontend/api/src/types.ts (WRONG)
   226→
   227→// CORRECT - use repo root
   228→repoRoot, _ := paths.RepoRoot()  // or strategy.GetWorktreePath()
   229→absPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, file)  // → /repo/api/src/types.ts (CORRECT)
   230→```
   231→
   232→This also affects path filtering. The `paths.ToRelativePath()` function rejects paths starting with `..`, so computing relative paths from cwd instead of repo root will filter out files in sibling directories:
   233→
   234→```go
   235→// WRONG - filters out sibling directory files
   236→cwd, _ := os.Getwd()  // /repo/frontend
   237→relPath := paths.ToRelativePath("/repo/api/file.ts", cwd)  // returns "" (filtered out as "../api/file.ts")
   238→
   239→// CORRECT - keeps all repo files
   240→repoRoot, _ := paths.RepoRoot()
   241→relPath := paths.ToRelativePath("/repo/api/file.ts", repoRoot)  // returns "api/file.ts"
   242→```
   243→
   244→**When to use `os.Getwd()`:** Only when you actually need the current directory (e.g., finding agent session directories that are cwd-relative).
   245→
   246→**When to use repo root:** Any time you're working with paths from git status, git diff, or any git-relative file list.
   247→
   248→Test case in `state_test.go`: `TestFilterAndNormalizePaths_SiblingDirectories` documents this bug pattern.
   249→
   250→### Session Strategies (`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/`)
   251→
   252→The CLI uses a strategy pattern for managing session data and checkpoints. Each strategy implements the `Strategy` interface defined in `strategy.go`.
   253→
   254→#### Core Interface
   255→All strategies implement:
   256→- `SaveChanges()` - Save session checkpoint (code + metadata)
   257→- `SaveTaskCheckpoint()` - Save subagent task checkpoint
   258→- `GetRewindPoints()` / `Rewind()` - List and restore to checkpoints
   259→- `GetSessionLog()` / `GetSessionInfo()` - Retrieve session data
   260→- `ListSessions()` / `GetSession()` - Session discovery
   261→
   262→#### Available Strategies
   263→
   264→| Strategy | Main Branch | Metadata Storage | Use Case |
   265→|----------|-------------|------------------|----------|
   266→| **manual-commit** (default) | Unchanged (no commits) | `entire/<HEAD-hash>-<worktreeHash>` branches + `entire/checkpoints/v1` | Recommended for most workflows |
   267→| **auto-commit** | Creates clean commits | Orphan `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch | Teams that want code commits from sessions |
   268→
   269→#### Strategy Details
   270→
   271→**Manual-Commit Strategy** (`manual_commit*.go`) - Default
   272→- **Does not modify** the active branch - no commits created on the working branch
   273→- Creates shadow branch `entire/<HEAD-commit-hash[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>` per base commit + worktree
   274→- **Worktree-specific branches** - each git worktree gets its own shadow branch namespace, preventing conflicts
   275→- **Supports multiple concurrent sessions** - checkpoints from different sessions in the same directory interleave on the same shadow branch
   276→- Session logs are condensed to permanent `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch on user commits
   277→- Builds git trees in-memory using go-git plumbing APIs
   278→- Rewind restores files from shadow branch commit tree (does not use `git reset`)
   279→- Tracks session state in `.git/entire-sessions/` (shared across worktrees)
   280→- **Shadow branch migration** - if user does stash/pull/rebase (HEAD changes without commit), shadow branch is automatically moved to new base commit
   281→- **Orphaned branch cleanup** - if a shadow branch exists without a corresponding session state file, it is automatically reset when a new session starts
   282→- PrePush hook can push `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch alongside user pushes
   283→- `AllowsMainBranch() = true` - safe to use on main/master since it never modifies commit history
   284→
   285→**Auto-Commit Strategy** (`auto_commit.go`)
   286→- Code commits to active branch with **clean history** (commits have `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer only)
   287→- Metadata stored on orphan `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch at sharded paths: `<id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/`
   288→- Uses `checkpoint.WriteCommitted()` for metadata storage
   289→- Checkpoint ID (12-hex-char) links code commits to metadata on `entire/checkpoints/v1`
   290→- Full rewind allowed if commit is only on current branch (not in main); otherwise logs-only
   291→- Rewind via `git reset --hard`
   292→- PrePush hook can push `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch alongside user pushes
   293→- `AllowsMainBranch() = true` - creates commits on active branch, safe to use on main/master
   294→
   295→#### Key Files
   296→
   297→- `strategy.go` - Interface definition and context structs (`SaveContext`, `RewindPoint`, etc.)
   298→- `registry.go` - Strategy registration/discovery (factory pattern with `Get()`, `List()`, `Default()`)
   299→- `common.go` - Shared helpers for metadata extraction, tree building, rewind validation, `ListCheckpoints()`
   300→- `session.go` - Session/checkpoint data structures
   301→- `push_common.go` - Shared PrePush logic for pushing `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch
   302→- `manual_commit.go` - Manual-commit strategy main implementation
   303→- `manual_commit_types.go` - Type definitions: `SessionState`, `CheckpointInfo`, `CondenseResult`
   304→- `manual_commit_session.go` - Session state management (load/save/list session states)
   305→- `manual_commit_condensation.go` - Condense logic for copying logs to `entire/checkpoints/v1`
   306→- `manual_commit_rewind.go` - Rewind implementation: file restoration from checkpoint trees
   307→- `manual_commit_git.go` - Git operations: checkpoint commits, tree building
   308→- `manual_commit_logs.go` - Session log retrieval and session listing
   309→- `manual_commit_hooks.go` - Git hook handlers (prepare-commit-msg, post-commit, pre-push)
   310→- `manual_commit_reset.go` - Shadow branch reset/cleanup functionality
   311→- `session_state.go` - Package-level session state functions (`LoadSessionState`, `SaveSessionState`, `ListSessionStates`, `FindMostRecentSession`)
   312→- `auto_commit.go` - Auto-commit strategy implementation
   313→- `hooks.go` - Git hook installation
   314→
   315→#### Checkpoint Package (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/`)
   316→- `checkpoint.go` - Data types (`Checkpoint`, `TemporaryCheckpoint`, `CommittedCheckpoint`)
   317→- `store.go` - `GitStore` struct wrapping git repository
   318→- `temporary.go` - Shadow branch operations (`WriteTemporary`, `ReadTemporary`, `ListTemporary`)
   319→- `committed.go` - Metadata branch operations (`WriteCommitted`, `ReadCommitted`, `ListCommitted`)
   320→
   321→#### Session Package (`cmd/entire/cli/session/`)
   322→- `session.go` - Session data types and interfaces
   323→- `state.go` - `StateStore` for managing `.git/entire-sessions/` files
   324→- `phase.go` - Session phase state machine (phases, events, transitions, actions)
   325→
   326→#### Session Phase State Machine
   327→
   328→Sessions track their lifecycle through phases managed by a state machine in `session/phase.go`:
   329→
   330→**Phases:** `ACTIVE`, `IDLE`, `ENDED`
   331→
   332→**Events:**
   333→- `TurnStart` - Agent begins a turn (UserPromptSubmit hook)
   334→- `TurnEnd` - Agent finishes a turn (Stop hook)
   335→- `GitCommit` - A git commit was made (PostCommit hook)
   336→- `SessionStart` - New session started
   337→- `SessionStop` - Session explicitly stopped
   338→
   339→**Key transitions:**
   340→- `IDLE + TurnStart → ACTIVE` - Agent starts working
   341→- `ACTIVE + TurnEnd → IDLE` - Agent finishes turn
   342→- `ACTIVE + GitCommit → ACTIVE` - User commits while agent is working (condense immediately)
   343→- `IDLE + GitCommit → IDLE` - User commits between turns (condense immediately)
   344→- `ENDED + GitCommit → ENDED` - Post-session commit (condense if files touched)
   345→
   346→The state machine emits **actions** (e.g., `ActionCondense`, `ActionUpdateLastInteraction`) that hook handlers dispatch to strategy-specific implementations.
   347→
   348→#### Metadata Structure
   349→
   350→**Shadow Strategy** - Shadow branches (`entire/<commit-hash[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>`):
   351→```
   352→.entire/metadata/<session-id>/
   353→├── full.jsonl               # Session transcript
   354→├── prompt.txt               # User prompts
   355→├── context.md               # Generated context
   356→└── tasks/<tool-use-id>/     # Task checkpoints
   357→    ├── checkpoint.json      # UUID mapping for rewind
   358→    └── agent-<id>.jsonl     # Subagent transcript
   359→```
   360→
   361→**Both Strategies** - Metadata branch (`entire/checkpoints/v1`) - sharded checkpoint format:
   362→```
   363→<checkpoint-id[:2]>/<checkpoint-id[2:]>/
   364→├── metadata.json            # CheckpointSummary (aggregated stats)
   365→├── 0/                       # First session (0-based indexing)
   366→│   ├── metadata.json        # Session-specific metadata
   367→│   ├── full.jsonl           # Session transcript
   368→│   ├── prompt.txt           # User prompts
   369→│   ├── context.md           # Generated context
   370→│   ├── content_hash.txt     # SHA256 of transcript
   371→│   └── tasks/<tool-use-id>/ # Task checkpoints (if applicable)
   372→│       ├── checkpoint.json  # UUID mapping
   373→│       └── agent-<id>.jsonl # Subagent transcript
   374→├── 1/                       # Second session (if multiple sessions)
   375→│   ├── metadata.json
   376→│   ├── full.jsonl
   377→│   └── ...
   378→└── ...
   379→```
   380→
   381→**Multi-session metadata.json format:**
   382→```json
   383→{
   384→  "checkpoint_id": "abc123def456",
   385→  "session_id": "2026-01-13-uuid",      // Current/latest session
   386→  "session_ids": ["2026-01-13-uuid1", "2026-01-13-uuid2"],  // All sessions
   387→  "session_count": 2,                    // Number of sessions in this checkpoint
   388→  "strategy": "manual-commit",
   389→  "created_at": "2026-01-13T12:00:00Z",
   390→  "files_touched": ["file1.txt", "file2.txt"]  // Merged from all sessions
   391→}
   392→```
   393→
   394→When multiple sessions are condensed to the same checkpoint (same base commit):
   395→- Sessions are stored in numbered subfolders using 0-based indexing (`0/`, `1/`, `2/`, etc.)
   396→- Latest session is always in the highest-numbered folder
   397→- `session_ids` array tracks all sessions, `session_count` increments
   398→
   399→**Session State** (filesystem, `.git/entire-sessions/`):
   400→```
   401→<session-id>.json            # Active session state (base_commit, checkpoint_count, etc.)
   402→```
   403→
   404→#### Checkpoint ID Linking
   405→
   406→Both strategies use a **12-hex-char random checkpoint ID** (e.g., `a3b2c4d5e6f7`) as the stable identifier linking user commits to metadata.
   407→
   408→**How checkpoint IDs work:**
   409→
   410→1. **Generated once per checkpoint**: Either when saving (auto-commit) or when condensing (manual-commit)
   411→
   412→2. **Added to user commits** via `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer:
   413→   - **Auto-commit**: Added programmatically when creating the commit
   414→   - **Manual-commit**: Added via `prepare-commit-msg` hook (user can remove it before committing)
   415→
   416→3. **Used for directory sharding** on `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch:
   417→   - Path format: `<id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/`
   418→   - Example: `a3b2c4d5e6f7` → `a3/b2c4d5e6f7/`
   419→   - Creates 256 shards to avoid directory bloat
   420→
   421→4. **Appears in commit subject** on `entire/checkpoints/v1` commits:
   422→   - Format: `Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7`
   423→   - Makes `git log entire/checkpoints/v1` readable and searchable
   424→
   425→**Bidirectional linking:**
   426→
   427→```
   428→User commit → Metadata:
   429→  Extract "Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7" trailer
   430→  → Read a3/b2c4d5e6f7/ directory from entire/checkpoints/v1 tree at HEAD
   431→
   432→Metadata → User commits:
   433→  Given checkpoint ID a3b2c4d5e6f7
   434→  → Search user branch history for commits with "Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7" trailer
   435→```
   436→
   437→Note: Commit subjects on `entire/checkpoints/v1` (e.g., `Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7`) are
   438→for human readability in `git log` only. The CLI always reads from the tree at HEAD.
   439→
   440→**Example:**
   441→```
   442→User's commit (on main branch):
   443→  "Implement login feature
   444→
   445→  Entire-Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7"
   446→       ↓ ↑
   447→       Linked via checkpoint ID
   448→       ↓ ↑
   449→entire/checkpoints/v1 commit:
   450→  Subject: "Checkpoint: a3b2c4d5e6f7"
   451→
   452→  Tree: a3/b2c4d5e6f7/
   453→    ├── metadata.json (checkpoint_id: "a3b2c4d5e6f7")
   454→    ├── full.jsonl (session transcript)
   455→    ├── prompt.txt
   456→    └── context.md
   457→```
   458→
   459→#### Commit Trailers
   460→
   461→**On user's active branch commits (both strategies):**
   462→- `Entire-Checkpoint: <checkpoint-id>` - 12-hex-char ID linking to metadata on `entire/checkpoints/v1`
   463→  - Auto-commit: Always added when creating commits
   464→  - Manual-commit: Added by hook; user can remove to skip linking
   465→
   466→**On shadow branch commits (`entire/<commit-hash[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>`) - manual-commit only:**
   467→- `Entire-Session: <session-id>` - Session identifier
   468→- `Entire-Metadata: <path>` - Path to metadata directory within the tree
   469→- `Entire-Task-Metadata: <path>` - Path to task metadata directory (for task checkpoints)
   470→- `Entire-Strategy: manual-commit` - Strategy that created the commit
   471→
   472→**On metadata branch commits (`entire/checkpoints/v1`) - both strategies:**
   473→
   474→Commit subject: `Checkpoint: <checkpoint-id>` (or custom subject for task checkpoints)
   475→
   476→Trailers:
   477→- `Entire-Session: <session-id>` - Session identifier
   478→- `Entire-Strategy: <strategy>` - Strategy name (manual-commit or auto-commit)
   479→- `Entire-Agent: <agent-name>` - Agent name (optional, e.g., "Claude Code")
   480→- `Ephemeral-branch: <branch>` - Shadow branch name (optional, manual-commit only)
   481→- `Entire-Metadata-Task: <path>` - Task metadata path (optional, for task checkpoints)
   482→
   483→**Note:** Both strategies keep active branch history **clean** - the only addition to user commits is the single `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer. Manual-commit never creates commits on the active branch (user creates them manually). Auto-commit creates commits but only adds the checkpoint trailer. All detailed session data (transcripts, prompts, context) is stored on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` orphan branch or shadow branches.
   484→
   485→#### Multi-Session Behavior
   486→
   487→**Concurrent Sessions:**
   488→- When a second session starts in the same directory while another has uncommitted checkpoints, a warning is shown
   489→- Both sessions can proceed - their checkpoints interleave on the same shadow branch
   490→- Each session's `RewindPoint` includes `SessionID` and `SessionPrompt` to help identify which checkpoint belongs to which session
   491→- On commit, all sessions are condensed together with archived sessions in numbered subfolders
   492→- Note: Different git worktrees have separate shadow branches (worktree-specific naming), so concurrent sessions in different worktrees do not conflict
   493→
   494→**Orphaned Shadow Branches:**
   495→- A shadow branch is "orphaned" if it exists but has no corresponding session state file
   496→- This can happen if the state file is manually deleted or lost
   497→- When a new session starts with an orphaned branch, the branch is automatically reset
   498→- If the existing session DOES have a state file (concurrent session in same directory), a `SessionIDConflictError` is returned
   499→
   500→**Shadow Branch Migration (Pull/Rebase):**
   501→- If user does stash → pull → apply (or rebase), HEAD changes but work isn't committed
   502→- The shadow branch would be orphaned at the old commit
   503→- Detection: base commit changed AND old shadow branch still exists (would be deleted if user committed)
   504→- Action: shadow branch is renamed from `entire/<old-hash>-<worktreeHash>` to `entire/<new-hash>-<worktreeHash>`
   505→- Session continues seamlessly with checkpoints preserved
   506→
   507→#### When Modifying Strategies
   508→- All strategies must implement the full `Strategy` interface
   509→- Register new strategies in `init()` using `Register()`
   510→- Test with `mise run test` - strategy tests are in `*_test.go` files
   511→- **Update this CLAUDE.md** when adding or modifying strategies to keep documentation current
   512→
   513→# Important Notes
   514→
   515→- **Before committing:** Follow the "Before Every Commit (REQUIRED)" checklist above - CI will fail without it
   516→- Integration tests: run `mise run test:integration` when changing integration test code
   517→- When adding new features, ensure they are well-tested and documented.
   518→- Always check for code duplication and refactor as needed.
   519→
   520→## Go Code Style
   521→- Write lint-compliant Go code on the first attempt. Before outputting Go code, mentally verify it passes `golangci-lint` (or your specific linter).
   522→- Follow standard Go idioms: proper error handling, no unused variables/imports, correct formatting (gofmt), meaningful names.
   523→- Handle all errors explicitly—don't leave them unchecked.
   524→- Reference `.golangci.yml` for enabled linters before writing Go code.
   525→
   526→## Accessibility
   527→
   528→The CLI supports an accessibility mode for users who rely on screen readers. This mode uses simpler text prompts instead of interactive TUI elements.
   529→
   530→### Environment Variable
   531→- `ACCESSIBLE=1` (or any non-empty value) enables accessibility mode
   532→- Users can set this in their shell profile (`.bashrc`, `.zshrc`) for persistent use
   533→
   534→### Implementation Guidelines
   535→
   536→When adding new interactive forms or prompts using `huh`:
   537→
   538→**In the `cli` package:**
   539→Use `NewAccessibleForm()` instead of `huh.NewForm()`:
   540→```go
   541→// Good - respects ACCESSIBLE env var
   542→form := NewAccessibleForm(
   543→    huh.NewGroup(
   544→        huh.NewSelect[string]().
   545→            Title("Choose an option").
   546→            Options(...).
   547→            Value(&choice),
   548→    ),
   549→)
   550→
   551→// Bad - ignores accessibility setting
   552→form := huh.NewForm(...)
   553→```
   554→
   555→**In the `strategy` package:**
   556→Use the `isAccessibleMode()` helper. Note that `WithAccessible()` is only available on forms, not individual fields, so wrap confirmations in a form:
   557→```go
   558→form := huh.NewForm(
   559→    huh.NewGroup(
   560→        huh.NewConfirm().
   561→            Title("Confirm action?").
   562→            Value(&confirmed),
   563→    ),
   564→)
   565→if isAccessibleMode() {
   566→    form = form.WithAccessible(true)
   567→}
   568→if err := form.Run(); err != nil { ... }
   569→```
   570→
   571→### Key Points
   572→- Always use the accessibility helpers for any `huh` forms/prompts
   573→- Test new interactive features with `ACCESSIBLE=1` to ensure they work
   574→- The accessible mode is documented in `--help` output
   575→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

```

     1→# Entire - CLI 
     2→
     3→This repo contains the CLI for Entire.
     4→
     5→## Architecture
     6→
     7→- CLI build with github.com/spf13/cobra and github.com/charmbracelet/huh 
     8→
     9→## Key Directories
    10→
    11→### Commands (`cmd/`)
    12→- `entire/`: Main CLI entry point
    13→- `entire/cli`: CLI utilities and helpers
    14→- `entire/cli/commands`: actual command implementations
    15→- `entire/cli/strategy`: strategy implementations - see section below
    16→- `entire/cli/checkpoint`: checkpoint storage abstractions (temporary and committed)
    17→- `entire/cli/session`: session state management
    18→- `entire/cli/integration_test`: integration tests (simulated hooks)
    19→- `entire/cli/e2e_test`: E2E tests with real agent calls (see E2E Tests section)
    20→
    21→## Tech Stack
    22→
    23→- Language: Go 1.25.x
    24→- Build tool: mise, go modules
    25→- Linting: golangci-lint
    26→
    27→## Development
    28→
    29→### Running Tests
    30→```bash
    31→mise run test
    32→```
    33→
    34→### Running Integration Tests
    35→```bash
    36→mise run test:integration
    37→```
    38→
    39→### Running All Tests (CI)
    40→```bash
    41→mise run test:ci
    42→```
    43→
    44→Integration tests use the `//go:build integration` build tag and are located in `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/`.
    45→
    46→### Running E2E Tests (Only When Explicitly Requested)
    47→
    48→**IMPORTANT: Do NOT run E2E tests proactively.** E2E tests make real API calls to Claude Code, which consume tokens and cost money. Only run them when the user explicitly asks for E2E testing.
    49→
    50→```bash
    51→# Requires Claude Code to be installed and authenticated
    52→E2E_AGENT=claude-code go test -tags=e2e ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...
    53→
    54→# Run a specific test
    55→E2E_AGENT=claude-code go test -tags=e2e -run TestE2E_BasicWorkflow ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...
    56→```
    57→
    58→E2E tests:
    59→- Use the `//go:build e2e` build tag
    60→- Located in `cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/`
    61→- Test real agent interactions (Claude Code creating files, committing, etc.)
    62→- Validate checkpoint scenarios documented in `docs/architecture/checkpoint-scenarios.md`
    63→- Support multiple agents via `E2E_AGENT` env var (currently `claude-code`, `gemini-cli` stub)
    64→
    65→**Environment variables:**
    66→- `E2E_AGENT` - Agent to test with (default: `claude-code`)
    67→- `E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL` - Claude model to use (default: `haiku` for cost efficiency)
    68→- `E2E_TIMEOUT` - Timeout per prompt (default: `2m`)
    69→
    70→### Test Parallelization
    71→
    72→**Always use `t.Parallel()` in tests.** Every top-level test function and subtest should call `t.Parallel()` unless it modifies process-global state (e.g., `os.Chdir()`).
    73→
    74→```go
    75→func TestFeature_Foo(t *testing.T) {
    76→    t.Parallel()
    77→    // ...
    78→}
    79→
    80→// Integration tests: RunForAllStrategies handles t.Parallel() for subtests internally,

    11→
    12→// TestE2E_CheckpointMetadata verifies that checkpoint metadata is correctly stored.
    13→func TestE2E_CheckpointMetadata(t *testing.T) {
    14→	t.Parallel()
    15→
    16→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    17→
    18→	// 1. Agent creates a file
    19→	t.Log("Step 1: Agent creating file")
    20→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateConfig.Prompt)
    21→	require.NoError(t, err)
    22→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    23→	AssertExpectedFilesExist(t, env, PromptCreateConfig)
    24→
    25→	// 2. Verify session created rewind points
    26→	t.Log("Step 2: Checking session rewind points")
    27→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    28→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points), 1, "Should have rewind points before commit")
    29→
    30→	// Note: Before commit, points are on the shadow branch
    31→	// They should have metadata directories set
    32→	for i, p := range points {
    33→		t.Logf("Rewind point %d: ID=%s, MetadataDir=%s, Message=%s",
    34→			i, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.MetadataDir, p.Message)
    35→	}
    36→
    37→	// 3. User commits
    38→	t.Log("Step 3: Committing changes")
    39→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add config file", "config.json")
    40→
    41→	// 4. Verify checkpoint trailer added
    42→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
    43→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint ID in commit history")
    44→	require.NotEmpty(t, checkpointID, "Should have checkpoint ID in commit")
    45→	t.Logf("Checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
    46→
    47→	// 5. Verify metadata branch has content
    48→	assert.True(t, env.BranchExists("entire/checkpoints/v1"),
    49→		"Metadata branch should exist after commit")
    50→
    51→	// 6. Verify rewind points now reference condensed metadata
    52→	t.Log("Step 4: Checking post-commit rewind points")
    53→	postPoints := env.GetRewindPoints()
    54→	// After commit, logs-only points from entire/checkpoints/v1 should exist
    55→	for i, p := range postPoints {
    56→		t.Logf("Post-commit point %d: ID=%s, IsLogsOnly=%v, CondensationID=%s",
    57→			i, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.IsLogsOnly, p.CondensationID)
    58→	}
    59→}
    60→
    61→// TestE2E_CheckpointIDFormat verifies checkpoint ID format is correct.
    62→func TestE2E_CheckpointIDFormat(t *testing.T) {
    63→	t.Parallel()
    64→
    65→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    66→
    67→	// 1. Agent makes changes

    69→	require.NoError(t, err)

    74→
    75→	// 3. Verify checkpoint ID format

    86→			"Checkpoint ID should be lowercase hex: got %c", c)
    87→	}
    88→}

The `TestE2E_AutoCommitStrategy` test is now strengthened with:

1. **Commit count tracking** - Verifies auto-commit actually creates commits
2. **Content validation** - `AssertHelloWorldProgram` validates the file content
3. **Checkpoint ID assertions** - `require.NoError` and format validation (12 hex chars)
4. **Metadata branch assertion** - `assert.True` instead of conditional logging
5. **Rewind point assertion** - Requires at least 1 rewind point
6. **`ValidateCheckpoint` call** - Comprehensive validation of:
   - Checkpoint ID linking
   - Strategy field is `"auto-commit"`
   - `files_touched` includes `hello.go`
   - Transcript contains `hello.go`
   - JSONL validity
   - Content hash verification

The test now has the same rigor as the manual-commit tests. The diagnostic warning about "No packages found" is expected since the file uses the `//go:build e2e` tag.

    12→// TestE2E_RewindToCheckpoint tests rewinding to a previous checkpoint.
    13→func TestE2E_RewindToCheckpoint(t *testing.T) {
    14→	t.Parallel()
    15→
    16→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    17→
    18→	// 1. Agent creates first file
    19→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating first file")
    20→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    21→	require.NoError(t, err)
    22→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    23→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
    24→
    25→	// Get first checkpoint
    26→	points1 := env.GetRewindPoints()
    27→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points1), 1)
    28→	firstPointID := points1[0].ID
    29→	t.Logf("First checkpoint: %s", safeIDPrefix(firstPointID))
    30→
    31→	// Save original content
    32→	originalContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    33→
    34→	// 2. Agent modifies the file
    35→	t.Log("Step 2: Modifying file")
    36→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptModifyHelloGo.Prompt)
    37→	require.NoError(t, err)
    38→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    39→
    40→	// Verify content changed
    41→	modifiedContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    42→	assert.NotEqual(t, originalContent, modifiedContent, "Content should have changed")
    43→	assert.Contains(t, modifiedContent, "E2E Test", "Should contain new message")
    44→
    45→	// Get second checkpoint
    46→	points2 := env.GetRewindPoints()
    47→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points2), 2, "Should have at least 2 checkpoints")
    48→	t.Logf("Now have %d checkpoints", len(points2))
    49→
    50→	// 3. Rewind to first checkpoint
    51→	t.Log("Step 3: Rewinding to first checkpoint")
    52→	err = env.Rewind(firstPointID)
    53→	require.NoError(t, err)
    54→
    55→	// 4. Verify content was restored
    56→	t.Log("Step 4: Verifying content restored")
    57→	restoredContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    58→	assert.Equal(t, originalContent, restoredContent, "Content should be restored to original")
    59→	assert.NotContains(t, restoredContent, "E2E Test", "Should not contain modified message")
    60→}
    61→
    62→// TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit tests rewinding to a checkpoint after user commits.
    63→func TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {
    64→	t.Parallel()
    65→
    66→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
    67→
    68→	// 1. Agent creates file
    69→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating file")
    70→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
    71→	require.NoError(t, err)
    72→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    73→
    74→	// Get checkpoint before commit
    75→	pointsBefore := env.GetRewindPoints()
    76→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(pointsBefore), 1)
    77→	preCommitPointID := pointsBefore[0].ID
    78→
    79→	// 2. User commits
    80→	t.Log("Step 2: Committing")
    81→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add hello world", "hello.go")
    82→
    83→	// 3. Agent modifies file (new session)
    84→	t.Log("Step 3: Modifying file after commit")
    85→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptModifyHelloGo.Prompt)
    86→	require.NoError(t, err)
    87→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
    88→
    89→	modifiedContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
    90→	require.Contains(t, modifiedContent, "E2E Test")
    91→
    92→	// 4. Get rewind points - should include both pre and post commit points
    93→	t.Log("Step 4: Getting rewind points")
    94→	points := env.GetRewindPoints()
    95→	t.Logf("Found %d rewind points", len(points))
    96→	for i, p := range points {
    97→		t.Logf("  Point %d: %s (logs_only=%v, condensation_id=%s)",
    98→			i, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.IsLogsOnly, p.CondensationID)
    99→	}
   100→
   101→	// 5. Rewind to pre-commit checkpoint
   102→	// After commit, the pre-commit checkpoint becomes logs-only because the shadow branch
   103→	// is condensed. Rewinding to a logs-only point should fail with a clear error.
   104→	t.Log("Step 5: Attempting rewind to pre-commit (logs-only) checkpoint")
   105→	err = env.Rewind(preCommitPointID)
   106→
   107→	// Verify the current file state regardless of rewind result
   108→	currentContent := env.ReadFile("hello.go")
   109→
   110→	if err != nil {
   111→		// Rewind failed as expected for logs-only checkpoint
   112→		t.Logf("Rewind to logs-only point failed as expected: %v", err)
   113→		// File should still have the modifications since rewind failed
   114→		assert.Contains(t, currentContent, "E2E Test",
   115→			"File should still have modifications after logs-only rewind failure")
   116→	} else {
   117→		// If rewind succeeded, the file might have been restored
   118→		// This could happen if the rewind point wasn't actually logs-only
   119→		t.Log("Rewind succeeded - checkpoint may not have been logs-only")
   120→	}
   121→}
   122→
   123→// TestE2E_RewindMultipleFiles tests rewinding changes across multiple files.
   124→func TestE2E_RewindMultipleFiles(t *testing.T) {
   125→	t.Parallel()
   126→
   127→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
   128→
   129→	// 1. Agent creates multiple files
   130→	t.Log("Step 1: Creating first file")
   131→	result, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)
   132→	require.NoError(t, err)
   133→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
   134→
   135→	// Get checkpoint after first file
   136→	points1 := env.GetRewindPoints()
   137→	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(points1), 1)
   138→	afterFirstFile := points1[0].ID
   139→
   140→	t.Log("Step 2: Creating second file")
   141→	result, err = env.RunAgent(PromptCreateCalculator.Prompt)
   142→	require.NoError(t, err)
   143→	AssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)
   144→
   145→	// Verify both files exist
   146→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"))
   147→	require.True(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"))
   148→
   149→	// 3. Rewind to after first file (before second)
   150→	t.Log("Step 3: Rewinding to after first file")
   151→	err = env.Rewind(afterFirstFile)
   152→	require.NoError(t, err)
   153→
   154→	// 4. Verify only first file exists
   155→	assert.True(t, env.FileExists("hello.go"), "hello.go should still exist")
   156→	assert.False(t, env.FileExists("calc.go"), "calc.go should be removed by rewind")
   157→}

Now I understand the flow. After a commit:
1. The shadow branch is condensed and deleted
2. `GetRewindPoints()` returns logs-only points with **different IDs** (user commit hashes)
3. The old shadow branch commit ID (`preCommitPointID`) is no longer valid
4. `entire rewind --to <old-id>` fails with "rewind point not found"

  "new_string": "// TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit tests that pre-commit checkpoint IDs become invalid after commit.\n//\n// Expected behavior:\n// 1. Before commit: checkpoints are on shadow branch with shadow branch commit IDs\n// 2. After commit: shadow branch is condensed and deleted\n// 3. New logs-only checkpoints appear with DIFFERENT IDs (user commit hashes)\n// 4. Attempting to rewind using the old shadow branch ID fails with \"not found\"\nfunc TestE2E_RewindAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\n\tenv := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, \"manual-commit\")\n\n\t// 1. Agent creates file\n\tt.Log(\"Step 1: Creating file\")\n\tresult, err := env.RunAgent(PromptCreateHelloGo.Prompt)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tAssertAgentSuccess(t, result, err)\n\n\t// Get checkpoint before commit - this is a shadow branch commit ID\n\tpointsBefore := env.GetRewindPoints()\n\trequire.GreaterOrEqual(t, len(pointsBefore), 1, \"Should have checkpoint before commit\")\n\tpreCommitPointID := pointsBefore[0].ID\n\tt.Logf(\"Pre-commit checkpoint ID (shadow branch): %s\", safeIDPrefix(preCommitPointID))\n\n\t// Verify this point is NOT logs-only (it's on shadow branch)\n\tassert.False(t, pointsBefore[0].IsLogsOnly, \"Pre-commit checkpoint should NOT be logs-only\")\n\n\t// 2. User commits - this condenses and deletes the shadow branch\n\tt.Log(\"Step 2: Committing (triggers condensation)\")\n\tenv.GitCommitWithShadowHooks(\"Add hello world\", \"hello.go\")\n\n\t// 3. Verify checkpoint was created\n\tcheckpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err, \"Should find checkpoint in commit history\")\n\tt.Logf(\"Committed checkpoint ID: %s\", checkpointID)\n\n\t// 4. Get rewind points after commit\n\tt.Log(\"Step 3: Getting rewind points after commit\")\n\tpointsAfter := env.GetRewindPoints()\n\tt.Logf(\"Found %d rewind points after commit\", len(pointsAfter))\n\n\t// Find the logs-only point (should be from the user commit)\n\tvar logsOnlyPoint *RewindPoint\n\tfor i, p := range pointsAfter {\n\t\tt.Logf(\"  Point %d: ID=%s, IsLogsOnly=%v, CondensationID=%s\",\n\t\t\ti, safeIDPrefix(p.ID), p.IsLogsOnly, p.CondensationID)\n\t\tif p.IsLogsOnly {\n\t\t\tpointCopy := p\n\t\t\tlogsOnlyPoint = &pointCopy\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\trequire.NotNil(t, logsOnlyPoint, \"Should have a logs-only point after commit\")\n\n\t// The logs-only point should have a DIFFERENT ID than the pre-commit shadow branch ID\n\tassert.NotEqual(t, preCommitPointID, logsOnlyPoint.ID,\n\t\t\"Logs-only point ID should differ from shadow branch ID\")\n\n\t// 5. Attempt to rewind to the OLD shadow branch ID - should fail\n\tt.Log(\"Step 4: Attempting rewind to old shadow branch ID (should fail)\")\n\terr = env.Rewind(preCommitPointID)\n\n\t// The old shadow branch was deleted, so the ID is no longer valid\n\tassert.Error(t, err, \"Rewind to deleted shadow branch ID should fail\")\n\tassert.Contains(t, err.Error(), \"not found\",\n\t\t\"Error should indicate the rewind point was not found\")\n\tt.Logf(\"Rewind correctly failed: %v\", err)\n}"
}

    12→
    13→// TestShadow_MidSessionRebaseMigration tests that when Claude performs a rebase
    14→// mid-session (via a tool call), the shadow branch is automatically migrated
    15→// to the new HEAD and subsequent checkpoints are saved correctly.
    16→//
    17→// This is a critical scenario because:
    18→// 1. Claude can run `git rebase` via the Bash tool
    19→// 2. No new prompt is submitted between the rebase and the next checkpoint
    20→// 3. Without migration in SaveChanges, checkpoints go to an orphaned shadow branch
    21→//
    22→// The test validates that:
    23→// - Checkpoints before rebase go to the original shadow branch
    24→// - Checkpoints after rebase go to the new (migrated) shadow branch
    25→// - The session state's BaseCommit is updated correctly
    26→func TestShadow_MidSessionRebaseMigration(t *testing.T) {
    27→	t.Parallel()
    28→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    29→	defer env.Cleanup()
    30→
    31→	// ========================================
    32→	// Phase 1: Setup - Create commits to rebase onto
    33→	// ========================================
    34→	env.InitRepo()
    35→
    36→	// Create initial commit on main
    37→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test Repository")
    38→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
    39→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
    40→
    41→	// Create a second commit on main (this will be our rebase target)
    42→	env.WriteFile("base.txt", "base content")
    43→	env.GitAdd("base.txt")
    44→	env.GitCommit("Add base file")
    45→	mainHead := env.GetHeadHash()
    46→
    47→	// Create feature branch from initial commit (before base.txt)
    48→	env.gitCheckout("HEAD~1")
    49→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/rebase-test")
    50→
    51→	// Initialize Entire after branch creation
    52→	env.InitEntire(strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
    53→
    54→	// Create a commit on feature branch
    55→	env.WriteFile("feature.txt", "feature content")
    56→	env.GitAdd("feature.txt")
    57→	env.GitCommit("Add feature file")
    58→
    59→	initialFeatureHead := env.GetHeadHash()
    60→	t.Logf("Initial feature HEAD: %s", initialFeatureHead[:7])
    61→	t.Logf("Main HEAD (rebase target): %s", mainHead[:7])
    62→
    63→	// ========================================
    64→	// Phase 2: Start session and create first checkpoint
    65→	// ========================================
    66→	t.Log("Phase 2: Starting session and creating first checkpoint")
    67→
    68→	session := env.NewSession()
    69→	if err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID); err != nil {
    70→		t.Fatalf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v", err)
    71→	}
    72→
    73→	// Create first file change
    74→	fileAContent := "package main\n\nfunc A() {}\n"
    75→	env.WriteFile("a.go", fileAContent)
    76→
    77→	session.CreateTranscript(
    78→		"Create function A",
    79→		[]FileChange{{Path: "a.go", Content: fileAContent}},
    80→	)

    20→// TestSessionIDConflict_OrphanedBranchIsReset tests that starting a new session
    21→// resets an orphaned shadow branch (one with no session state file).
    22→func TestSessionIDConflict_OrphanedBranchIsReset(t *testing.T) {
    23→	t.Parallel()
    24→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    25→	defer env.Cleanup()
    26→
    27→	// Setup
    28→	env.InitRepo()
    29→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test")
    30→	env.GitAdd("README.md")
    31→	env.GitCommit("Initial commit")
    32→
    33→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/test")
    34→	env.InitEntire(strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
    35→
    36→	baseHead := env.GetHeadHash()
    37→	shadowBranch := env.GetShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseHead)
    38→
    39→	// Create a session and checkpoint (this creates the shadow branch)
    40→	session1 := env.NewSession()
    41→	if err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session1.ID); err != nil {
    42→		t.Fatalf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit (session1) failed: %v", err)
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	env.WriteFile("test.txt", "content")
    46→	session1.CreateTranscript("Add test file", []FileChange{{Path: "test.txt", Content: "content"}})
    47→	if err := env.SimulateStop(session1.ID, session1.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
    48→		t.Fatalf("SimulateStop (session1) failed: %v", err)
    49→	}
    50→
    51→	// Verify shadow branch exists
    52→	if !env.BranchExists(shadowBranch) {
    53→		t.Fatalf("Shadow branch %s should exist after first session", shadowBranch)
    54→	}
    55→	t.Logf("Created shadow branch: %s", shadowBranch)
    56→
    57→	// Clear the session state file but keep the shadow branch
    58→	// This simulates an orphaned shadow branch scenario
    59→	sessionStateDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions")
    60→	entries, err := os.ReadDir(sessionStateDir)
    61→	if err != nil {
    62→		t.Fatalf("Failed to read session state dir: %v", err)
    63→	}
    64→	for _, entry := range entries {
    65→		if strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".json") {
    66→			if err := os.Remove(filepath.Join(sessionStateDir, entry.Name())); err != nil {
    67→				t.Fatalf("Failed to remove session state file: %v", err)
    68→			}
    69→		}
    70→	}
    71→
    72→	// Try to start a new session - should succeed by resetting the orphaned branch
    73→	session2 := env.NewSession()
    74→	err = env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session2.ID)
    75→	// Expect success - orphaned branch is reset
    76→	if err != nil {
    77→		t.Errorf("Expected success when starting new session with orphaned shadow branch, got: %v", err)
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	// Verify the new session can create checkpoints

> AGENT

Based on my analysis of the integration tests vs e2e tests, here are my suggestions:

## Scenarios Currently in Integration Tests but NOT in E2E

| Scenario | Integration Test File | Worth E2E? | Reasoning |
|----------|----------------------|------------|-----------|
| **Worktree commits** | `worktree_test.go` | ❌ No | Tests go-git behavior, not agent behavior. Integration tests verify commits persist. |
| **Mid-session rebase** | `mid_session_rebase_test.go` | ⚠️ Maybe | Rare edge case where Claude runs `git rebase`. High cost, low likelihood. |
| **Subagent/Task checkpoints** | `subagent_checkpoints_test.go` | ✅ Yes | Tests real Task tool usage. Could catch transcript parsing issues with real agent output. |
| **Session resume** | `resume_test.go` | ✅ Yes | Tests `--resume` flag with real agent context restoration. |
| **Subdirectory execution** | `subdirectory_test.go` | ✅ Yes | Agents often run from subdirs. Could catch path handling bugs. |
| **Session conflicts/orphans** | `session_conflict_test.go` | ❌ No | Internal recovery mechanism. Simulated hooks sufficient. |
| **Untracked file preservation** | `manual_commit_untracked_files_test.go` | ⚠️ Maybe | Could be valuable but complex setup. |
| **Hook logging** | `hook_logging_test.go` | ❌ No | Internal logging, not agent behavior. |
| **Git author preservation** | `git_author_test.go` | ❌ No | Git config, not agent behavior. |

## My Recommendations (in priority order)

**1. Subagent/Task checkpoints** - Most valuable
- Real Claude uses the Task tool to spawn subagents
- Integration tests simulate the transcript format
- E2E could catch issues with actual Task tool JSON structure

**2. Subdirectory execution** - Medium value
- Common real-world scenario (e.g., running from `frontend/` subdirectory)
- Could catch path resolution bugs that simulated hooks might miss

**3. Session resume** - Medium value
- Tests that `claude --resume` + Entire hooks work together
- Would validate transcript restoration with real agent

**4. Mid-session rebase** - Low value (expensive)
- Very edge case scenario
- Would require prompting Claude to run `git rebase`
- High token cost for rare scenario

Would you like me to implement any of these? I'd suggest starting with **subagent checkpoints** since it tests a real Claude feature (the Task tool) that's hard to fully simulate.

   109→
   110→		// Step 3: PostTask - creates final task checkpoint
   111→		err = env.SimulatePostTask(PostTaskInput{
   112→			SessionID:      session.ID,
   113→			TranscriptPath: session.TranscriptPath,
   114→			ToolUseID:      taskToolUseID,
   115→			AgentID:        "agent-123",
   116→		})
   117→		if err != nil {
   118→			t.Fatalf("SimulatePostTask failed: %v", err)
   119→		}
   120→
   121→		// Verify pre-task file is cleaned up
   122→		if _, err := os.Stat(preTaskFile); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
   123→			t.Error("Pre-task file should be removed after PostTask")
   124→		}
   125→
   126→		// Verify checkpoints are stored in final location (strategy-specific)
   127→		verifyCheckpointStorage(t, env, strategyName, session.ID, taskToolUseID)
   128→	})
   129→}
   130→
   131→// TestSubagentCheckpoints_NoFileChanges tests that PostTodo is skipped when no file changes
   132→func TestSubagentCheckpoints_NoFileChanges(t *testing.T) {
   133→	t.Parallel()
   134→	RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
   135→		// Create a session
   136→		session := env.NewSession()
   137→
   138→		// Create transcript
   139→		session.CreateTranscript("Quick task", []FileChange{})
   140→
   141→		// Simulate user prompt submit
   142→		err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)
   143→		if err != nil {
   144→			t.Fatalf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v", err)
   145→		}
   146→
   147→		// Create pre-task file to simulate subagent context
   148→		taskToolUseID := "toolu_01TaskNoChanges"
   149→		err = env.SimulatePreTask(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath, taskToolUseID)
   150→		if err != nil {
   151→			t.Fatalf("SimulatePreTask failed: %v", err)
   152→		}
   153→
   154→		// Get git log before PostTodo
   155→		beforeCommits := env.GetGitLog()
   156→
   157→		// Call PostTodo WITHOUT making any file changes
   158→		err = env.SimulatePostTodo(PostTodoInput{
   159→			SessionID:      session.ID,
   160→			TranscriptPath: session.TranscriptPath,
   161→			ToolUseID:      "toolu_01TodoWriteNoChange",
   162→			Todos: []Todo{
   163→				{Content: "Some task", Status: "pending", ActiveForm: "Doing task"},
   164→			},
   165→		})
   166→		if err != nil {
   167→			t.Fatalf("SimulatePostTodo should not fail: %v", err)
   168→		}
   169→
   170→		// Get git log after PostTodo
   171→		afterCommits := env.GetGitLog()
   172→
   173→		// Verify no new commits were created
   174→		if len(afterCommits) != len(beforeCommits) {
   175→			t.Errorf("Expected no new commits when no file changes, before=%d after=%d", len(beforeCommits), len(afterCommits))
   176→		}
   177→	})
   178→}
   179→
   180→// TestSubagentCheckpoints_PostTaskNoFileChanges tests that PostTask is skipped when no file changes
   181→// and the pre-task state is still cleaned up.
   182→func TestSubagentCheckpoints_PostTaskNoFileChanges(t *testing.T) {
   183→	t.Parallel()
   184→	RunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
   185→		// Create a session
   186→		session := env.NewSession()
   187→
   188→		// Create transcript (no file changes in transcript either)
   189→		session.CreateTranscript("Quick task with no file changes", []FileChange{})
   190→
   191→		// Simulate user prompt submit
   192→		err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)
   193→		if err != nil {
   194→			t.Fatalf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v", err)
   195→		}
   196→
   197→		// Create pre-task file to simulate subagent context
   198→		taskToolUseID := "toolu_01TaskNoFileChanges"
   199→		err = env.SimulatePreTask(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath, taskToolUseID)
   200→		if err != nil {
   201→			t.Fatalf("SimulatePreTask failed: %v", err)
   202→		}
   203→
   204→		// Verify pre-task file was created
   205→		preTaskFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", "pre-task-"+taskToolUseID+".json")
   206→		if _, err := os.Stat(preTaskFile); os.IsNotExist(err) {
   207→			t.Fatal("pre-task file should exist after SimulatePreTask")
   208→		}
   209→
   210→		// Get git log before PostTask
   211→		beforeCommits := env.GetGitLog()
   212→
   213→		// Call PostTask WITHOUT making any file changes
   214→		err = env.SimulatePostTask(PostTaskInput{
   215→			SessionID:      session.ID,
   216→			TranscriptPath: session.TranscriptPath,
   217→			ToolUseID:      taskToolUseID,
   218→			AgentID:        "agent-no-changes",
   219→		})
   220→		if err != nil {
   221→			t.Fatalf("SimulatePostTask should not fail: %v", err)
   222→		}
   223→
   224→		// Get git log after PostTask
   225→		afterCommits := env.GetGitLog()
   226→
   227→		// Verify no new commits were created on the main branch
   228→		if len(afterCommits) != len(beforeCommits) {
   229→			t.Errorf("Expected no new commits when no file changes, before=%d after=%d", len(beforeCommits), len(afterCommits))
   230→		}
   231→
   232→		// Verify pre-task file is cleaned up even though no checkpoint was created
   233→		if _, err := os.Stat(preTaskFile); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
   234→			t.Error("Pre-task file should be removed after PostTask even with no file changes")
   235→		}
   236→	})
   237→}
