    36→func filesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowBranchName string, headCommit *object.Commit, filesTouched []string) bool {
    37→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
    38→
    39→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
    40→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
    41→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
    42→		touchedSet[f] = true
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
    46→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
    47→	if err != nil {
    48→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
    49→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    50→		)
    51→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's content)
    55→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
    56→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: shadow branch not found, falling back to filename check",
    59→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
    60→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    61→		)
    62→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
    66→	if err != nil {
    67→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to filename check",
    68→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    69→		)
    70→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    71→	}
    72→
    73→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
    74→	if err != nil {
    75→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to filename check",
    76→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    77→		)
    78→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    79→	}
    80→
    81→	// Get the parent commit tree to determine if files are modified vs newly created.
    82→	// For modified files (exist in parent), we count as overlap regardless of content
    83→	// because the user is editing the session's work.
    84→	// For newly created files (don't exist in parent), we check content to detect
    85→	// the "reverted and replaced" scenario where user deleted session's work and
    86→	// created something completely different.
    87→	var parentTree *object.Tree
    88→	if headCommit.NumParents() > 0 {
    89→		if parent, err := headCommit.Parent(0); err == nil {
    90→			if pTree, err := parent.Tree(); err == nil {
    91→				parentTree = pTree
    92→			}
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	// Check each file in filesTouched
    97→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
    98→		// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)
    99→		headFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)
   100→		if err != nil {
   101→			// File not in HEAD commit. This happens when:
   102→			// - The session created/modified the file but user deleted it before committing
   103→			// - The file was staged as a deletion (git rm)
   104→			// In both cases, the session's work on this file is not in the commit,
   105→			// so it doesn't contribute to overlap. Continue checking other files.
   106→			continue
   107→		}
   108→
   109→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file
   110→		isModified := false
   111→		if parentTree != nil {
   112→			if _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {
   113→				isModified = true
   114→			}
   115→		}
   116→
   117→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   118→		if isModified {
   119→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   120→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   121→			)
   122→			return true
   123→		}
   124→
   125→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   126→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   127→		if err != nil {
   128→			// File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
   129→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
   130→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   131→			)
   132→			continue
   133→		}
   134→
   135→		// Compare by hash (blob hash) - exact content match required for new files
   136→		if headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {
   137→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   138→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   139→				slog.String("hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   140→			)
   141→			return true
   142→		}
   143→
   144→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   145→			slog.String("file", filePath),
   146→			slog.String("head_hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   147→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   148→		)
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   152→		slog.Int("files_checked", len(filesTouched)),
   153→	)
   154→	return false
   155→}

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

> AGENT

Fixed. The change:

**Before:** If file was in `filesTouched` but missing from shadow tree → `return true` (assume overlap)

**After:** If file was in `filesTouched` but missing from shadow tree → `continue` (can't verify, skip)

This aligns `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent` with `filesOverlapWithContent` which already handled this case correctly at line 126-133.

    13→
    14→// Content-aware overlap detection for checkpoint management.
    15→//
    16→// These functions determine whether a commit contains session-related work by comparing
    17→// file content (not just filenames) against the shadow branch. This enables accurate
    18→// detection of the "reverted and replaced" scenario where a user:
    19→// 1. Reverts session changes (e.g., git checkout -- file.txt)
    20→// 2. Creates completely different content in the same file
    21→// 3. Commits the new content
    22→//
    23→// In this scenario, the commit should NOT get a checkpoint trailer because the
    24→// session's work was discarded, not incorporated.
    25→//
    26→// The key distinction:
    27→// - Modified files (exist in parent commit): Always count as overlap, regardless of
    28→//   content changes. The user is editing session's work.
    29→// - New files (don't exist in parent): Require content match against shadow branch.
    30→//   If content differs completely, the session's work was likely reverted & replaced.
    31→
    32→// filesOverlapWithContent checks if any file in filesTouched overlaps with the committed
    33→// content, using content-aware comparison to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
    34→//
    35→// This is used in PostCommit to determine if a session has work in the commit.
    36→func filesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowBranchName string, headCommit *object.Commit, filesTouched []string) bool {
    37→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
    38→
    39→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
    40→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
    41→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
    42→		touchedSet[f] = true
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
    46→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
    47→	if err != nil {
    48→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
    49→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    50→		)
    51→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's content)
    55→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
    56→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: shadow branch not found, falling back to filename check",
    59→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
    60→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    61→		)
    62→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
    66→	if err != nil {
    67→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to filename check",
    68→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    69→		)
    70→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    71→	}
    72→
    73→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
    74→	if err != nil {
    75→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to filename check",
    76→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    77→		)
    78→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    79→	}
    80→
    81→	// Get the parent commit tree to determine if files are modified vs newly created.
    82→	// For modified files (exist in parent), we count as overlap regardless of content
    83→	// because the user is editing the session's work.
    84→	// For newly created files (don't exist in parent), we check content to detect
    85→	// the "reverted and replaced" scenario where user deleted session's work and
    86→	// created something completely different.
    87→	var parentTree *object.Tree
    88→	if headCommit.NumParents() > 0 {
    89→		if parent, err := headCommit.Parent(0); err == nil {
    90→			if pTree, err := parent.Tree(); err == nil {
    91→				parentTree = pTree
    92→			}
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	// Check each file in filesTouched
    97→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
    98→		// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)
    99→		headFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)
   100→		if err != nil {
   101→			// File not in HEAD commit. This happens when:
   102→			// - The session created/modified the file but user deleted it before committing
   103→			// - The file was staged as a deletion (git rm)
   104→			// In both cases, the session's work on this file is not in the commit,
   105→			// so it doesn't contribute to overlap. Continue checking other files.
   106→			continue
   107→		}
   108→
   109→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file
   110→		isModified := false
   111→		if parentTree != nil {
   112→			if _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {
   113→				isModified = true
   114→			}
   115→		}
   116→
   117→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   118→		if isModified {
   119→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   120→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   121→			)
   122→			return true
   123→		}
   124→
   125→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   126→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   127→		if err != nil {
   128→			// File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
   129→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
   130→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   131→			)
   132→			continue
   133→		}
   134→
   135→		// Compare by hash (blob hash) - exact content match required for new files
   136→		if headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {
   137→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   138→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   139→				slog.String("hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   140→			)
   141→			return true
   142→		}
   143→
   144→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   145→			slog.String("file", filePath),
   146→			slog.String("head_hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   147→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   148→		)
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   152→		slog.Int("files_checked", len(filesTouched)),
   153→	)
   154→	return false
   155→}
   156→
   157→// stagedFilesOverlapWithContent checks if any staged file overlaps with filesTouched,
   158→// distinguishing between modified files (always overlap) and new files (check content).
   159→//
   160→// For modified files (already exist in HEAD), we count as overlap because the user
   161→// is editing the session's work. For new files (don't exist in HEAD), we require
   162→// content match to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
   163→//
   164→// This is used in PrepareCommitMsg for carry-forward scenarios.
   165→func stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowTree *object.Tree, stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   166→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   167→
   168→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
   169→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   170→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   171→		touchedSet[f] = true
   172→	}
   173→
   174→	// Get HEAD tree to determine if files are being modified or newly created
   175→	head, err := repo.Head()
   176→	if err != nil {
   177→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD, falling back to filename check",
   178→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   179→		)
   180→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   181→	}
   182→	headCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   183→	if err != nil {
   184→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD commit, falling back to filename check",
   185→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   186→		)
   187→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   188→	}
   189→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   190→	if err != nil {
   191→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
   192→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   193→		)
   194→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	// Get the git index to access staged file hashes
   198→	idx, err := repo.Storer.Index()
   199→	if err != nil {
   200→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get index, falling back to filename check",
   201→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   202→		)
   203→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   204→	}
   205→
   206→	// Build a map of index entries for O(1) lookup (avoid O(n*m) nested loop)
   207→	indexEntries := make(map[string]plumbing.Hash, len(idx.Entries))
   208→	for _, entry := range idx.Entries {
   209→		indexEntries[entry.Name] = entry.Hash
   210→	}
   211→
   212→	// Check each staged file
   213→	for _, stagedPath := range stagedFiles {
   214→		if !touchedSet[stagedPath] {
   215→			continue // Not in filesTouched, skip
   216→		}
   217→
   218→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in HEAD) or new file
   219→		_, headErr := headTree.File(stagedPath)
   220→		isModified := headErr == nil
   221→
   222→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   223→		if isModified {
   224→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   225→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   226→			)
   227→			return true
   228→		}
   229→
   230→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   231→		stagedHash, found := indexEntries[stagedPath]
   232→		if !found {
   233→			continue // Not in index (shouldn't happen but be safe)
   234→		}
   235→
   236→		// Get file from shadow branch tree
   237→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(stagedPath)
   238→		if err != nil {
   239→			// File not in shadow branch - can't verify content overlap.
   240→			// Don't assume overlap just because it's in filesTouched.
   241→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: file not in shadow tree, skipping",
   242→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   243→			)
   244→			continue
   245→		}
   246→
   247→		// Compare hashes - exact match means file is unchanged
   248→		if stagedHash == shadowFile.Hash {
   249→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   250→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   251→				slog.String("hash", stagedHash.String()),
   252→			)
   253→			return true
   254→		}
   255→
   256→		// Hashes differ - check if there's significant content overlap.
   257→		// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from
   258→		// "reverted and replaced" (user wrote completely different content).
   259→		shadowContent, shadowErr := shadowFile.Contents()
   260→		if shadowErr != nil {
   261→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read shadow content",
   262→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   263→				slog.String("error", shadowErr.Error()),
   264→			)
   265→			continue
   266→		}
   267→
   268→		// Read staged content from object store
   269→		stagedBlob, blobErr := repo.BlobObject(stagedHash)
   270→		if blobErr != nil {
   271→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read staged blob",
   272→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   273→				slog.String("error", blobErr.Error()),
   274→			)
   275→			continue
   276→		}
   277→		stagedReader, readerErr := stagedBlob.Reader()
   278→		if readerErr != nil {
   279→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get staged reader",
   280→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   281→				slog.String("error", readerErr.Error()),
   282→			)
   283→			continue
   284→		}
   285→		stagedBytes, readErr := io.ReadAll(stagedReader)
   286→		_ = stagedReader.Close() // Best effort close
   287→		if readErr != nil {
   288→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read staged content",
   289→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   290→				slog.String("error", readErr.Error()),
   291→			)
   292→			continue
   293→		}
   294→		stagedContent := string(stagedBytes)
   295→
   296→		// Check for significant content overlap
   297→		if hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent) {
   298→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file has partial overlap (partial staging)",
   299→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   300→			)
   301→			return true
   302→		}
   303→
   304→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file has no significant overlap (reverted & replaced)",
   305→			slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   306→			slog.String("staged_hash", stagedHash.String()),
   307→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   308→		)
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   312→		slog.Int("staged_files", len(stagedFiles)),
   313→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   314→	)
   315→	return false
   316→}
   317→
   318→// hasOverlappingFiles checks if any file in stagedFiles appears in filesTouched.
   319→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   320→func hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   321→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   322→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   323→		touchedSet[f] = true
   324→	}
   325→
   326→	for _, staged := range stagedFiles {
   327→		if touchedSet[staged] {
   328→			return true
   329→		}
   330→	}
   331→	return false
   332→}
   333→
   334→// filesWithRemainingAgentChanges returns files from filesTouched that still have
   335→// uncommitted agent changes. This is used for carry-forward after partial commits.
   336→//
   337→// A file has remaining agent changes if:
   338→//   - It wasn't committed at all (not in committedFiles), OR
   339→//   - It was committed but the committed content doesn't match the shadow branch
   340→//     (user committed partial changes, e.g., via git add -p)
   341→//
   342→// Falls back to file-level subtraction if shadow branch is unavailable.
   343→func filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(
   344→	repo *git.Repository,
   345→	shadowBranchName string,
   346→	headCommit *object.Commit,
   347→	filesTouched []string,
   348→	committedFiles map[string]struct{},
   349→) []string {
   350→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   351→
   352→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
   353→	commitTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   354→	if err != nil {
   355→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get commit tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   356→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   357→		)
   358→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   359→	}
   360→
   361→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's full content)
   362→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   363→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   364→	if err != nil {
   365→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: shadow branch not found, falling back to file subtraction",
   366→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
   367→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   368→		)
   369→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   373→	if err != nil {
   374→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to file subtraction",
   375→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   376→		)
   377→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   378→	}
   379→
   380→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   381→	if err != nil {
   382→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   383→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   384→		)
   385→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   386→	}
   387→
   388→	var remaining []string
   389→
   390→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
   391→		// If file wasn't committed at all, it definitely has remaining changes
   392→		if _, wasCommitted := committedFiles[filePath]; !wasCommitted {
   393→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   394→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not committed, keeping",
   395→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   396→			)
   397→			continue
   398→		}
   399→
   400→		// File was committed - check if committed content matches shadow branch
   401→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   402→		if err != nil {
   403→			// File not in shadow branch - nothing to carry forward for this file
   404→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in shadow branch, skipping",
   405→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   406→			)
   407→			continue
   408→		}
   409→
   410→		commitFile, err := commitTree.File(filePath)
   411→		if err != nil {
   412→			// File not in commit tree (deleted?) - keep it if it's in shadow
   413→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   414→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in commit tree but in shadow, keeping",
   415→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   416→			)
   417→			continue
   418→		}
   419→
   420→		// Compare hashes - if different, there are still uncommitted agent changes
   421→		if commitFile.Hash != shadowFile.Hash {
   422→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   423→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content mismatch, keeping for carry-forward",
   424→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   425→				slog.String("commit_hash", commitFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   426→				slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   427→			)
   428→		} else {
   429→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content fully committed",
   430→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   431→			)
   432→		}
   433→	}
   434→
   435→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: result",
   436→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   437→		slog.Int("committed_files", len(committedFiles)),
   438→		slog.Int("remaining_files", len(remaining)),
   439→	)
   440→
   441→	return remaining
   442→}
   443→
   444→// subtractFilesByName returns files from filesTouched that are NOT in committedFiles.
   445→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   446→func subtractFilesByName(filesTouched []string, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) []string {
   447→	var remaining []string
   448→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   449→		if _, committed := committedFiles[f]; !committed {
   450→			remaining = append(remaining, f)
   451→		}
   452→	}
   453→	return remaining
   454→}
   455→
   456→// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.
   457→// Returns true if the contents share at least one non-trivial line.
   458→// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from
   459→// "reverted and replaced" (user wrote completely different content).
   460→//
   461→// The function filters out trivial lines (boilerplate like package declarations,
   462→// import statements, empty braces, etc.) because these commonly appear in many
   463→// files and don't indicate meaningful overlap.
   464→func hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {
   465→	// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content
   466→	shadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)
   467→
   468→	// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content
   469→	stagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)
   470→
   471→	// If both have no significant lines, hashes differ so no meaningful overlap
   472→	if len(shadowLines) == 0 && len(stagedLines) == 0 {
   473→		return false
   474→	}
   475→
   476→	// If shadow has significant lines but staged doesn't, no overlap
   477→	if len(shadowLines) > 0 && len(stagedLines) == 0 {
   478→		return false
   479→	}
   480→
   481→	// If shadow has no significant lines but staged does, no overlap
   482→	if len(shadowLines) == 0 && len(stagedLines) > 0 {
   483→		return false
   484→	}
   485→
   486→	// Check if any staged line matches a shadow line
   487→	for line := range stagedLines {
   488→		if shadowLines[line] {
   489→			return true
   490→		}
   491→	}
   492→
   493→	return false
   494→}
   495→
   496→// extractSignificantLines returns a set of significant lines from content.
   497→// Lines are trimmed and must be at least 10 characters.
   498→//
   499→// This simple length-based filter works well because:
   500→// - Most structural boilerplate is short: `{`, `}`, `});`, `} else {` (all < 10 chars)
   501→// - Long lines (>= 10 chars) are usually meaningful regardless of language
   502→// - No language-specific pattern matching needed
   503→func extractSignificantLines(content string) map[string]bool {
   504→	lines := make(map[string]bool)
   505→	for _, line := range splitLines([]byte(content)) {
   506→		trimmed := trimLine(line)
   507→		if len(trimmed) >= 10 {
   508→			lines[trimmed] = true
   509→		}
   510→	}
   511→	return lines
   512→}
   513→
   514→// trimLine removes leading and trailing whitespace from a line.
   515→func trimLine(line string) string {
   516→	start := 0
   517→	end := len(line)
   518→	for start < end && (line[start] == ' ' || line[start] == '\t') {
   519→		start++
   520→	}
   521→	for end > start && (line[end-1] == ' ' || line[end-1] == '\t') {
   522→		end--
   523→	}
   524→	return line[start:end]
   525→}
   526→

> AGENT

Now I understand the issue. Looking at the test case:

**Shadow file (agent created):**
```go
package main

func C() {}
```

**Manual file (user created after deleting):**
```go
package main

// Completely different implementation
func C() { panic("manual") }
```

With our length-based filtering (>= 10 chars):
- Both files have `package main` (12 chars) - this is matching!

The problem is that `package main` is common boilerplate that appears in both files, causing a false positive. The `hasSignificantContentOverlap` function returns `true` because of this single shared line.

The fix should require **at least 2 matching significant lines** to count as overlap, not just 1. A single matching line like `package main` doesn't indicate the user kept agent work.

> TOOL

id: toolu_01UHTUNyqf8rjfGHiTFjCXC6
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/content_overlap.go",
  "old_string": "// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.\n// Returns true if the contents share at least one non-trivial line.\n// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from\n// \"reverted and replaced\" (user wrote completely different content).\n//\n// The function filters out trivial lines (boilerplate like package declarations,\n// import statements, empty braces, etc.) because these commonly appear in many\n// files and don't indicate meaningful overlap.\nfunc hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {\n\t// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content\n\tshadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)\n\n\t// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content\n\tstagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)\n\n\t// If both have no significant lines, hashes differ so no meaningful overlap\n\tif len(shadowLines) == 0 && len(stagedLines) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// If shadow has significant lines but staged doesn't, no overlap\n\tif len(shadowLines) > 0 && len(stagedLines) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// If shadow has no significant lines but staged does, no overlap\n\tif len(shadowLines) == 0 && len(stagedLines) > 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// Check if any staged line matches a shadow line\n\tfor line := range stagedLines {\n\t\tif shadowLines[line] {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}",
  "new_string": "// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.\n// Returns true if the contents share at least 2 non-trivial lines.\n// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from\n// \"reverted and replaced\" (user wrote completely different content).\n//\n// We require at least 2 matching lines because a single match (like \"package main\")\n// is likely common boilerplate, not evidence that user kept agent work.\n//\n// The function filters out trivial lines (short lines < 10 chars like braces,\n// empty lines, etc.) because these commonly appear in many files and don't\n// indicate meaningful overlap.\nfunc hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {\n\t// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content\n\tshadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)\n\n\t// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content\n\tstagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)\n\n\t// If either has no significant lines, no meaningful overlap possible\n\tif len(shadowLines) == 0 || len(stagedLines) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// Count matching lines - require at least 2 to count as overlap.\n\t// A single match (e.g., \"package main\") is likely boilerplate, not\n\t// evidence that user kept agent work.\n\tmatchCount := 0\n\tfor line := range stagedLines {\n\t\tif shadowLines[line] {\n\t\t\tmatchCount++\n\t\t\tif matchCount >= 2 {\n\t\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}"
}

    33→	require.NoError(t, err)
    34→	_, err = wt.Commit("Initial commit", &git.CommitOptions{

   230→	require.NoError(t, err)
   231→	headCommit, err := wt.Commit("Add file A only", &git.CommitOptions{
   232→		Author: &object.Signature{Name: "Test", Email: "test@test.com", When: time.Now()},
   233→	})
   234→	require.NoError(t, err)
   235→
   236→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(headCommit)
   237→	require.NoError(t, err)
   238→
   239→	shadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit("abc1234", "e3b0c4")
   240→	committedFiles := map[string]struct{}{"fileA.txt": {}}
   241→
   242→	// fileB was not committed - should be in remaining
   243→	remaining := filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(repo, shadowBranch, commit, []string{"fileA.txt", "fileB.txt"}, committedFiles)
   244→	assert.Equal(t, []string{"fileB.txt"}, remaining, "Uncommitted file should be in remaining")
   245→}
   246→
   247→// TestFilesWithRemainingAgentChanges_FullyCommitted tests that files committed with
   248→// matching content are NOT in the remaining list.
   249→func TestFilesWithRemainingAgentChanges_FullyCommitted(t *testing.T) {
   250→	t.Parallel()
   251→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   252→
   253→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   254→	require.NoError(t, err)
   255→
   256→	content := []byte("exact same content")
   257→
   258→	// Create shadow branch with file
   259→	createShadowBranchWithContent(t, repo, "def5678", "e3b0c4", map[string][]byte{
   260→		"test.txt": content,
   261→	})
   262→
   263→	// Commit the file with SAME content
   264→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "test.txt")
   265→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, content, 0o644))
   266→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   267→	require.NoError(t, err)
   268→	_, err = wt.Add("test.txt")
   269→	require.NoError(t, err)
   270→	headCommit, err := wt.Commit("Add file with same content", &git.CommitOptions{
   271→		Author: &object.Signature{Name: "Test", Email: "test@test.com", When: time.Now()},
   272→	})
   273→	require.NoError(t, err)
   274→
   275→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(headCommit)
   276→	require.NoError(t, err)
   277→
   278→	shadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit("def5678", "e3b0c4")
   279→	committedFiles := map[string]struct{}{"test.txt": {}}
   280→
   281→	// File was fully committed - should NOT be in remaining
   282→	remaining := filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(repo, shadowBranch, commit, []string{"test.txt"}, committedFiles)
   283→	assert.Empty(t, remaining, "Fully committed file should not be in remaining")
   284→}

   286→// TestFilesWithRemainingAgentChanges_PartialCommit tests that files committed with
   287→// different content (partial commit via git add -p) ARE in the remaining list.
   288→func TestFilesWithRemainingAgentChanges_PartialCommit(t *testing.T) {
   289→	t.Parallel()
   290→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   291→
   292→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   293→	require.NoError(t, err)
   294→
   295→	// Shadow branch has the full agent content
   296→	fullContent := []byte("line 1\nline 2\nline 3\nline 4\n")
   297→	createShadowBranchWithContent(t, repo, "ghi9012", "e3b0c4", map[string][]byte{
   298→		"test.txt": fullContent,
   299→	})
   300→
   301→	// User commits only partial content (simulating git add -p)
   302→	partialContent := []byte("line 1\nline 2\n")
   303→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "test.txt")
   304→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, partialContent, 0o644))
   305→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   306→	require.NoError(t, err)
   307→	_, err = wt.Add("test.txt")
   308→	require.NoError(t, err)
   309→	headCommit, err := wt.Commit("Partial commit", &git.CommitOptions{
   310→		Author: &object.Signature{Name: "Test", Email: "test@test.com", When: time.Now()},
   311→	})
   312→	require.NoError(t, err)
   313→
   314→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(headCommit)
   315→	require.NoError(t, err)
   316→
   317→	shadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit("ghi9012", "e3b0c4")
   318→	committedFiles := map[string]struct{}{"test.txt": {}}
   319→
   320→	// Content doesn't match - file should be in remaining (has more agent changes)
   321→	remaining := filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(repo, shadowBranch, commit, []string{"test.txt"}, committedFiles)
   322→	assert.Equal(t, []string{"test.txt"}, remaining, "Partially committed file should be in remaining")
   323→}

   325→// TestFilesWithRemainingAgentChanges_NoShadowBranch tests fallback to file-level subtraction.
   326→func TestFilesWithRemainingAgentChanges_NoShadowBranch(t *testing.T) {
   327→	t.Parallel()
   328→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   329→
   330→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   331→	require.NoError(t, err)
   332→
   333→	// Create a commit without any shadow branch
   334→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "test.txt")
   335→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, []byte("content"), 0o644))
   336→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   337→	require.NoError(t, err)
   338→	_, err = wt.Add("test.txt")
   339→	require.NoError(t, err)
   340→	headCommit, err := wt.Commit("Test commit", &git.CommitOptions{
   341→		Author: &object.Signature{Name: "Test", Email: "test@test.com", When: time.Now()},
   342→	})
   343→	require.NoError(t, err)
   344→
   345→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(headCommit)
   346→	require.NoError(t, err)
   347→
   348→	// Non-existent shadow branch should fall back to file-level subtraction
   349→	committedFiles := map[string]struct{}{"test.txt": {}}
   350→	remaining := filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(repo, "entire/nonexistent-e3b0c4", commit, []string{"test.txt", "other.txt"}, committedFiles)
   351→
   352→	// With file-level subtraction: test.txt is in committedFiles, other.txt is not
   353→	assert.Equal(t, []string{"other.txt"}, remaining, "Fallback should use file-level subtraction")
   354→}

   356→// TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_ModifiedFile tests that a modified file
   357→// (exists in HEAD) always counts as overlap.
   358→func TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_ModifiedFile(t *testing.T) {
   359→	t.Parallel()
   360→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   361→
   362→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   363→	require.NoError(t, err)
   364→
   365→	// Initial file is created by setupGitRepo
   366→	// Modify it and stage
   367→	testFile := filepath.Join(dir, "test.txt")
   368→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(testFile, []byte("modified content"), 0o644))
   369→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   370→	require.NoError(t, err)
   371→	_, err = wt.Add("test.txt")
   372→	require.NoError(t, err)
   373→
   374→	// Create shadow branch (content doesn't matter for modified files)
   375→	createShadowBranchWithContent(t, repo, "abc1234", "e3b0c4", map[string][]byte{
   376→		"test.txt": []byte("shadow content"),
   377→	})
   378→
   379→	// Get shadow tree
   380→	shadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit("abc1234", "e3b0c4")
   381→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranch), true)
   382→	require.NoError(t, err)
   383→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   384→	require.NoError(t, err)
   385→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   386→	require.NoError(t, err)
   387→
   388→	// Modified file should count as overlap regardless of content
   389→	result := stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo, shadowTree, []string{"test.txt"}, []string{"test.txt"})
   390→	assert.True(t, result, "Modified file should always count as overlap")
   391→}

   430→// TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_NewFile_ContentMismatch tests that a new file
   431→// with different content does NOT count as overlap (reverted & replaced scenario).
   432→func TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_NewFile_ContentMismatch(t *testing.T) {
   433→	t.Parallel()
   434→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   435→
   436→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   437→	require.NoError(t, err)
   438→
   439→	// Create a NEW file with different content than shadow branch
   440→	newFile := filepath.Join(dir, "newfile.txt")
   441→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(newFile, []byte("user replaced content"), 0o644))
   442→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
   443→	require.NoError(t, err)
   444→	_, err = wt.Add("newfile.txt")
   445→	require.NoError(t, err)
   446→
   447→	// Create shadow branch with DIFFERENT content (agent's original)
   448→	createShadowBranchWithContent(t, repo, "ghi9012", "e3b0c4", map[string][]byte{
   449→		"newfile.txt": []byte("agent original content"),
   450→	})
   451→
   452→	// Get shadow tree
   453→	shadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit("ghi9012", "e3b0c4")
   454→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranch), true)
   455→	require.NoError(t, err)
   456→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   457→	require.NoError(t, err)
   458→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   459→	require.NoError(t, err)
   460→
   461→	// New file with different content should NOT count as overlap
   462→	result := stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo, shadowTree, []string{"newfile.txt"}, []string{"newfile.txt"})
   463→	assert.False(t, result, "New file with mismatched content should not count as overlap")
   464→}

   466→// TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_NoOverlap tests that non-overlapping files
   467→// return false.
   468→func TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_NoOverlap(t *testing.T) {
   469→	t.Parallel()
   470→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   471→
   472→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   473→	require.NoError(t, err)
   474→
   475→	// Stage a file NOT in filesTouched
   476→	otherFile := filepath.Join(dir, "other.txt")
   477→	require.NoError(t, os.WriteFile(otherFile, []byte("other content"), 0o644))
   478→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()

   480→	_, err = wt.Add("other.txt")
   481→	require.NoError(t, err)
   482→
   483→	// Create shadow branch
   484→	createShadowBranchWithContent(t, repo, "jkl3456", "e3b0c4", map[string][]byte{
   485→		"session.txt": []byte("session content"),
   486→	})
   487→
   488→	// Get shadow tree
   489→	shadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit("jkl3456", "e3b0c4")
   490→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranch), true)
   491→	require.NoError(t, err)
   492→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   493→	require.NoError(t, err)
   494→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   495→	require.NoError(t, err)
   496→
   497→	// Staged file "other.txt" is not in filesTouched "session.txt"
   498→	result := stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo, shadowTree, []string{"other.txt"}, []string{"session.txt"})
   499→	assert.False(t, result, "Non-overlapping files should return false")
   500→}
   501→
   502→// createShadowBranchWithContent creates a shadow branch with the given file contents.
   503→// This helper directly uses go-git APIs to avoid paths.RepoRoot() dependency.
   504→//
   505→//nolint:unparam // worktreeID is kept as a parameter for flexibility even if tests currently use same value
   506→func createShadowBranchWithContent(t *testing.T, repo *git.Repository, baseCommit, worktreeID string, fileContents map[string][]byte) {
   507→	t.Helper()
   508→
   509→	shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   510→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   511→
   512→	// Get HEAD for base tree
   513→	head, err := repo.Head()
   514→	require.NoError(t, err)
   515→
   516→	headCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   517→	require.NoError(t, err)
   518→
   519→	baseTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   520→	require.NoError(t, err)
   521→
   522→	// Flatten existing tree into map
   523→	entries := make(map[string]object.TreeEntry)
   524→	err = checkpoint.FlattenTree(repo, baseTree, "", entries)
   525→	require.NoError(t, err)
   526→
   527→	// Add/update files with provided content
   528→	for filePath, content := range fileContents {
   529→		// Create blob with content
   530→		blob := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   531→		blob.SetType(plumbing.BlobObject)
   532→		blob.SetSize(int64(len(content)))
   533→		writer, err := blob.Writer()
   534→		require.NoError(t, err)
   535→		_, err = writer.Write(content)
   536→		require.NoError(t, err)
   537→		err = writer.Close()
   538→		require.NoError(t, err)
   539→
   540→		blobHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(blob)
   541→		require.NoError(t, err)
   542→
   543→		entries[filePath] = object.TreeEntry{
   544→			Name: filePath,
   545→			Mode: filemode.Regular,
   546→			Hash: blobHash,
   547→		}
   548→	}
   549→
   550→	// Build tree from entries
   551→	treeHash, err := checkpoint.BuildTreeFromEntries(repo, entries)
   552→	require.NoError(t, err)
   553→
   554→	// Create commit
   555→	commit := &object.Commit{
   556→		TreeHash: treeHash,
   557→		Message:  "Test checkpoint",
   558→		Author: object.Signature{
   559→			Name:  "Test",
   560→			Email: "test@test.com",
   561→			When:  time.Now(),
   562→		},
   563→		Committer: object.Signature{
   564→			Name:  "Test",
   565→			Email: "test@test.com",
   566→			When:  time.Now(),
   567→		},
   568→	}
   569→
   570→	commitObj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   571→	err = commit.Encode(commitObj)
   572→	require.NoError(t, err)
   573→
   574→	commitHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(commitObj)
   575→	require.NoError(t, err)
   576→
   577→	// Create branch reference
   578→	newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(refName, commitHash)
   579→	err = repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef)
   580→	require.NoError(t, err)
   581→}
   582→
   583→// TestExtractSignificantLines tests the line extraction with length-based filtering.
   584→// Lines must be >= 10 characters after trimming whitespace.
   585→func TestExtractSignificantLines(t *testing.T) {
   586→	t.Parallel()
   587→
   588→	tests := []struct {
   589→		name     string
   590→		content  string
   591→		wantKeys []string // lines that should be in the result
   592→		wantNot  []string // lines that should NOT be in the result
   593→	}{
   594→		{
   595→			name: "go function",
   596→			content: `package main
   597→
   598→func hello() {
   599→	fmt.Println("hello world")
   600→	return
   601→}`,
   602→			wantKeys: []string{
   603→				"package main",               // 12 chars
   604→				"func hello() {",             // 14 chars
   605→				`fmt.Println("hello world")`, // 26 chars
   606→			},
   607→			wantNot: []string{
   608→				"}",      // 1 char
   609→				"return", // 6 chars
   610→			},
   611→		},
   612→		{
   613→			name: "python function",
   614→			content: `def calculate(x, y):
   615→    result = x + y
   616→    print(f"Result: {result}")
   617→    return result`,
   618→			wantKeys: []string{
   619→				"def calculate(x, y):",       // 20 chars
   620→				"result = x + y",             // 14 chars
   621→				`print(f"Result: {result}")`, // 25 chars
   622→				"return result",              // 13 chars
   623→			},
   624→			wantNot: []string{},
   625→		},
   626→		{
   627→			name: "javascript",
   628→			content: `const handler = async (req) => {
   629→  const data = await fetch(url);
   630→  return data.json();
   631→};`,
   632→			wantKeys: []string{
   633→				"const handler = async (req) => {", // 32 chars
   634→				"const data = await fetch(url);",   // 30 chars
   635→				"return data.json();",              // 19 chars
   636→			},
   637→			wantNot: []string{
   638→				"};", // 2 chars
   639→			},
   640→		},
   641→		{
   642→			name: "short lines filtered",
   643→			content: `a = 1
   644→b = 2
   645→longVariableName = 42`,
   646→			wantKeys: []string{
   647→				"longVariableName = 42", // 21 chars
   648→			},
   649→			wantNot: []string{
   650→				"a = 1", // 5 chars
   651→				"b = 2", // 5 chars
   652→			},
   653→		},
   654→		{
   655→			name: "structural lines filtered by length",
   656→			content: `{
   657→  });
   658→  ]);
   659→  },
   660→}`,
   661→			wantKeys: []string{},
   662→			wantNot: []string{
   663→				"{",   // 1 char
   664→				"});", // 3 chars
   665→				"]);", // 3 chars
   666→				"},",  // 2 chars
   667→				"}",   // 1 char
   668→			},
   669→		},
   670→		{
   671→			name: "regex and special chars kept if long enough",
   672→			content: `short
   673→/^[a-z0-9]+@[a-z]+\.[a-z]{2,}$/
   674→x`,
   675→			wantKeys: []string{
   676→				"/^[a-z0-9]+@[a-z]+\\.[a-z]{2,}$/", // 32 chars - kept even though mostly non-alpha
   677→			},
   678→			wantNot: []string{
   679→				"short", // 5 chars
   680→				"x",     // 1 char
   681→			},
   682→		},
   683→	}
   684→
   685→	for _, tt := range tests {
   686→		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
   687→			t.Parallel()
   688→			result := extractSignificantLines(tt.content)
   689→
   690→			for _, want := range tt.wantKeys {
   691→				if !result[want] {
   692→					t.Errorf("extractSignificantLines() missing expected line: %q", want)
   693→				}
   694→			}
   695→
   696→			for _, notWant := range tt.wantNot {
   697→				if result[notWant] {
   698→					t.Errorf("extractSignificantLines() should not contain: %q", notWant)
   699→				}
   700→			}
   701→		})
   702→	}
   703→}
   704→
   705→// TestHasSignificantContentOverlap tests the content overlap detection logic.
   706→func TestHasSignificantContentOverlap(t *testing.T) {
   707→	t.Parallel()
   708→
   709→	tests := []struct {
   710→		name          string
   711→		stagedContent string
   712→		shadowContent string
   713→		wantOverlap   bool
   714→	}{
   715→		{
   716→			name:          "both have matching significant lines",
   717→			stagedContent: "this is a significant line\nshort",
   718→			shadowContent: "this is a significant line\nother",
   719→			wantOverlap:   true,
   720→		},
   721→		{
   722→			name:          "no matching significant lines",
   723→			stagedContent: "completely different content here",
   724→			shadowContent: "this is the shadow content now",
   725→			wantOverlap:   false,
   726→		},
   727→		{
   728→			name:          "both have only short lines - no significant content",
   729→			stagedContent: "a = 1\nb = 2\nc = 3",
   730→			shadowContent: "x = 1\ny = 2\nz = 3",
   731→			wantOverlap:   false,
   732→		},
   733→		{
   734→			name:          "shadow has significant lines but staged has none",
   735→			stagedContent: "a = 1\nb = 2",
   736→			shadowContent: "this is significant content from shadow",
   737→			wantOverlap:   false,
   738→		},
   739→		{
   740→			name:          "staged has significant lines but shadow has none",
   741→			stagedContent: "this is significant content from staged",
   742→			shadowContent: "x = 1\ny = 2",
   743→			wantOverlap:   false,
   744→		},
   745→		{
   746→			name:          "empty strings",
   747→			stagedContent: "",
   748→			shadowContent: "",
   749→			wantOverlap:   false,
   750→		},
   751→		{
   752→			name:          "partial match - some lines overlap",
   753→			stagedContent: "unique staged line here\nshared significant line",
   754→			shadowContent: "unique shadow line here\nshared significant line",
   755→			wantOverlap:   true,
   756→		},
   757→	}
   758→
   759→	for _, tt := range tests {
   760→		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
   761→			t.Parallel()
   762→			got := hasSignificantContentOverlap(tt.stagedContent, tt.shadowContent)
   763→			if got != tt.wantOverlap {
   764→				t.Errorf("hasSignificantContentOverlap() = %v, want %v", got, tt.wantOverlap)
   765→			}
   766→		})
   767→	}

   773→
   774→	tests := []struct {

   788→		{"empty string", "", ""},
   789→		{"spaces in middle preserved", "hello world", "hello world"},
   790→		{"tabs in middle preserved", "hello\tworld", "hello\tworld"},
   791→	}
   792→

> AGENT

Fixed. Here's a summary of the changes:

**Root Cause:** The test was failing because when the user reverted a file and created different content, the single shared line `package main` (common Go boilerplate) was causing a false positive in the content overlap detection.

**Fix:** Changed `hasSignificantContentOverlap` to require **at least 2 matching significant lines** instead of 1. A single matching line like `package main` is likely common boilerplate, not evidence that the user kept agent work.

**Changes made:**

1. **`content_overlap.go`** - Updated `hasSignificantContentOverlap` to count matching lines and return `true` only when `matchCount >= 2`

2. **`content_overlap_test.go`** - Updated unit tests to reflect the new requirement:
   - Added test case "only one matching significant line - no overlap"
   - Added test case "single shared line like package main - no overlap (boilerplate)"
   - Added test case "multiple shared lines - overlap (user kept agent work)"

This combined with the earlier fix (changing `return true` to `continue` when shadow file is missing) ensures accurate detection of the "reverted and replaced" scenario.

> DEVELOPER

Subagent tests can panic on agent errors

Low Severity

When RunAgentWithTools returns an error, result can be nil, but the test still reads result.Stdout/result.Stderr. That dereference can panic and abort the suite, especially with non-claude-code runners that return nil, err, making failures look like crashes instead of assertion results.

> TOOL

    98→// TestE2E_SubagentCheckpoint_CommitFlow tests that task checkpoints are properly
    99→// handled when the user commits changes that include subagent-created files.
   100→func TestE2E_SubagentCheckpoint_CommitFlow(t *testing.T) {
   101→	t.Parallel()
   102→
   103→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, "manual-commit")
   104→
   105→	// 1. Run prompt that may trigger Task tool
   106→	t.Log("Step 1: Running prompt that may use Task tool")
   107→	result, err := env.RunAgentWithTools(PromptUseTaskTool.Prompt, []string{
   108→		"Edit", "Read", "Write", "Bash", "Glob", "Grep", "Task",
   109→	})
   110→
   111→	if err != nil {
   112→		t.Logf("Agent completed with error: %v", err)
   113→	}
   114→	t.Logf("Agent output: %s", truncateMessage(result.Stdout, 200))
   115→
   116→	// 2. Check what files were created
   117→	var filesToCommit []string
   118→	if env.FileExists("subagent_output.txt") {
   119→		filesToCommit = append(filesToCommit, "subagent_output.txt")
   120→		t.Log("Found subagent_output.txt")
   121→	}
   122→
   123→	// If no files created, the test can't continue meaningfully
   124→	if len(filesToCommit) == 0 {
   125→		t.Log("No files created by agent - skipping commit flow test")
   126→		t.Log("This may happen if Task tool wasn't used or failed")
   127→		return
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	// 3. Get rewind points before commit
   131→	pointsBefore := env.GetRewindPoints()
   132→	taskCheckpointsBefore := countTaskCheckpoints(pointsBefore)
   133→	t.Logf("Task checkpoints before commit: %d", taskCheckpointsBefore)
   134→
   135→	// 4. Commit the changes
   136→	t.Log("Step 2: Committing changes")
   137→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add subagent output", filesToCommit...)
   138→
   139→	// 5. Verify checkpoint was created
   140→	checkpointID, err := env.GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory()
   141→	require.NoError(t, err, "Should find checkpoint in commit history")
   142→	t.Logf("Checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
   143→
   144→	// 6. Validate checkpoint on metadata branch
   145→	env.ValidateCheckpoint(CheckpointValidation{
   146→		CheckpointID:              checkpointID,
   147→		Strategy:                  "manual-commit",
   148→		FilesTouched:              filesToCommit,
   149→		ExpectedTranscriptContent: []string{"subagent_output.txt"},
   150→	})
   151→
   152→	// 7. Verify rewind points after commit
   153→	pointsAfter := env.GetRewindPoints()
   154→	t.Logf("Rewind points after commit: %d", len(pointsAfter))
   155→
   156→	// Check for logs-only point from the commit
   157→	var logsOnlyFound bool
   158→	for _, p := range pointsAfter {
   159→		if p.IsLogsOnly {
   160→			logsOnlyFound = true
   161→			t.Logf("Found logs-only point: %s", safeIDPrefix(p.ID))
   162→		}
   163→	}
   164→	assert.True(t, logsOnlyFound, "Should have logs-only point after commit")
   165→}
   166→
   167→// countTaskCheckpoints counts how many task checkpoints are in the list.
   168→func countTaskCheckpoints(points []RewindPoint) int {
   169→	count := 0
   170→	for _, p := range points {
   171→		if p.IsTaskCheckpoint {
   172→			count++
   173→		}
   174→	}
   175→	return count
   176→}
   177→
   178→// truncateMessage truncates a message to maxLen characters, adding "..." if truncated.
   179→func truncateMessage(msg string, maxLen int) string {
   180→	// Remove newlines for cleaner logging
   181→	msg = strings.ReplaceAll(msg, "\n", " ")
   182→	if len(msg) > maxLen {
   183→		return msg[:maxLen] + "..."
   184→	}
   185→	return msg
   186→}

> AGENT

Fixed. Both test functions now check if `result` is nil before accessing its fields, preventing potential nil pointer dereference panics when the agent runner returns `(nil, err)`.

> DEVELOPER

Any suggestions for: 

Short files lose partial-overlap detection

Medium Severity

hasSignificantContentOverlap now requires at least two matching lines, and extractSignificantLines drops all lines shorter than 10 chars. For compact files, partial commits can keep real agent content but still produce zero “significant” matches, so stagedFilesOverlapWithContent returns false and the commit is treated as unrelated.

> AGENT

Good catch. For small/compact files, the 2-line requirement can cause false negatives where real agent content goes undetected.

A reasonable fix: if either file has very few significant lines (e.g., ≤ 2), treat any single match as overlap. The 2-line requirement is mainly to filter out coincidental boilerplate in larger files.

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/content_overlap.go",
  "old_string": "// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.\n// Returns true if the contents share at least 2 non-trivial lines.\n// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from\n// \"reverted and replaced\" (user wrote completely different content).\n//\n// We require at least 2 matching lines because a single match (like \"package main\")\n// is likely common boilerplate, not evidence that user kept agent work.\n//\n// The function filters out trivial lines (short lines < 10 chars like braces,\n// empty lines, etc.) because these commonly appear in many files and don't\n// indicate meaningful overlap.\nfunc hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {\n\t// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content\n\tshadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)\n\n\t// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content\n\tstagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)\n\n\t// If either has no significant lines, no meaningful overlap possible\n\tif len(shadowLines) == 0 || len(stagedLines) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// Count matching lines - require at least 2 to count as overlap.\n\t// A single match (e.g., \"package main\") is likely boilerplate, not\n\t// evidence that user kept agent work.\n\tmatchCount := 0\n\tfor line := range stagedLines {\n\t\tif shadowLines[line] {\n\t\t\tmatchCount++\n\t\t\tif matchCount >= 2 {\n\t\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}",
  "new_string": "// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.\n// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from\n// \"reverted and replaced\" (user wrote completely different content).\n//\n// For larger files, we require at least 2 matching lines because a single match\n// (like \"package main\") is likely common boilerplate. For small files (≤ 2\n// significant lines in either file), any single match counts as overlap since\n// there aren't many lines to match anyway.\n//\n// The function filters out trivial lines (short lines < 10 chars like braces,\n// empty lines, etc.) because these commonly appear in many files and don't\n// indicate meaningful overlap.\nfunc hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {\n\t// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content\n\tshadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)\n\n\t// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content\n\tstagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)\n\n\t// If either has no significant lines, no meaningful overlap possible\n\tif len(shadowLines) == 0 || len(stagedLines) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// For small files, any single match is meaningful since there aren't\n\t// many lines to match anyway. The 2-line requirement is mainly to filter\n\t// out coincidental boilerplate (like \"package main\") in larger files.\n\tisSmallFile := len(shadowLines) <= 2 || len(stagedLines) <= 2\n\trequiredMatches := 2\n\tif isSmallFile {\n\t\trequiredMatches = 1\n\t}\n\n\t// Count matching lines\n\tmatchCount := 0\n\tfor line := range stagedLines {\n\t\tif shadowLines[line] {\n\t\t\tmatchCount++\n\t\t\tif matchCount >= requiredMatches {\n\t\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}"
}

> AGENT

The test failures show that `<= 2` is too generous. Files with exactly 2 significant lines can still have the "package main" false positive problem. The small file exception should only apply when it's impossible to require 2 matches (i.e., one side has < 2 significant lines).

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_016uhjT7Q7MRn5u3o8W59hXy
```json

  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/content_overlap.go",
  "old_string": "\t// For small files, any single match is meaningful since there aren't\n\t// many lines to match anyway. The 2-line requirement is mainly to filter\n\t// out coincidental boilerplate (like \"package main\") in larger files.\n\tisSmallFile := len(shadowLines) <= 2 || len(stagedLines) <= 2\n\trequiredMatches := 2\n\tif isSmallFile {\n\t\trequiredMatches = 1\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// For very small files (0-1 significant lines), any single match is meaningful\n\t// since requiring 2 matches would be impossible. The 2-line requirement is\n\t// mainly to filter out coincidental boilerplate (like \"package main\") in\n\t// larger files where we CAN require multiple matches.\n\tisVerySmallFile := len(shadowLines) < 2 || len(stagedLines) < 2\n\trequiredMatches := 2\n\tif isVerySmallFile {\n\t\trequiredMatches = 1\n\t}"
}

> AGENT

Fixed. The `hasSignificantContentOverlap` function now handles very small files (0-1 significant lines) by requiring only 1 match instead of 2, since requiring 2 matches would be impossible for files with a single significant line.

**Summary of changes:**
- Changed threshold from `<= 2` to `< 2` significant lines to trigger the small file exception
- This preserves the "package main" false positive fix for files with 2+ significant lines
- While allowing accurate overlap detection for truly compact files where 2 matches are impossible

     1→//go:build e2e
     2→
     3→package e2e
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"bytes"
     7→	"context"
     8→	"errors"
     9→	"fmt"
    10→	"os"
    11→	"os/exec"
    12→	"time"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// AgentNameClaudeCode is the name for Claude Code agent.
    16→const AgentNameClaudeCode = "claude-code"
    17→
    18→// AgentNameGeminiCLI is the name for Gemini CLI agent.
    19→const AgentNameGeminiCLI = "gemini-cli"
    20→
    21→// AgentRunner abstracts invoking a coding agent for e2e tests.
    22→// This follows the multi-agent pattern from cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go.
    23→type AgentRunner interface {
    24→	// Name returns the agent name (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini-cli")
    25→	Name() string
    26→
    27→	// IsAvailable checks if the agent CLI is installed and authenticated
    28→	IsAvailable() (bool, error)
    29→
    30→	// RunPrompt executes a prompt and returns the result
    31→	RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error)
    32→
    33→	// RunPromptWithTools executes with specific allowed tools
    34→	RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error)
    35→}
    36→
    37→// AgentResult holds the result of an agent invocation.
    38→type AgentResult struct {
    39→	Stdout   string
    40→	Stderr   string
    41→	ExitCode int
    42→	Duration time.Duration
    43→}
    44→
    45→// AgentRunnerConfig holds configuration for agent runners.
    46→type AgentRunnerConfig struct {
    47→	Model   string        // Model to use (e.g., "haiku" for Claude)
    48→	Timeout time.Duration // Timeout per prompt
    49→}
    50→
    51→// NewAgentRunner creates an agent runner based on the agent name.
    52→//
    53→//nolint:ireturn // factory pattern intentionally returns interface
    54→func NewAgentRunner(name string, config AgentRunnerConfig) AgentRunner {
    55→	switch name {
    56→	case AgentNameClaudeCode:
    57→		return NewClaudeCodeRunner(config)
    58→	case AgentNameGeminiCLI:
    59→		return NewGeminiCLIRunner(config)
    60→	default:
    61→		// Return a runner that reports as unavailable
    62→		return &unavailableRunner{name: name}
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// unavailableRunner is returned for unknown agent names.
    67→type unavailableRunner struct {
    68→	name string
    69→}
    70→
    71→func (r *unavailableRunner) Name() string { return r.name }
    72→
    73→func (r *unavailableRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
    74→	return false, fmt.Errorf("unknown agent: %s", r.name)
    75→}
    76→
    77→func (r *unavailableRunner) RunPrompt(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string) (*AgentResult, error) {
    78→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent %s is not available", r.name)
    79→}
    80→
    81→func (r *unavailableRunner) RunPromptWithTools(_ context.Context, _ string, _ string, _ []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
    82→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent %s is not available", r.name)
    83→}
    84→
    85→// ClaudeCodeRunner implements AgentRunner for Claude Code CLI.
    86→type ClaudeCodeRunner struct {
    87→	Model        string
    88→	Timeout      time.Duration
    89→	AllowedTools []string
    90→}
    91→
    92→// NewClaudeCodeRunner creates a new Claude Code runner with the given config.
    93→func NewClaudeCodeRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *ClaudeCodeRunner {
    94→	model := config.Model
    95→	if model == "" {
    96→		model = os.Getenv("E2E_CLAUDE_MODEL")
    97→		if model == "" {
    98→			model = "haiku"
    99→		}
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	timeout := config.Timeout
   103→	if timeout == 0 {
   104→		if envTimeout := os.Getenv("E2E_TIMEOUT"); envTimeout != "" {
   105→			if parsed, err := time.ParseDuration(envTimeout); err == nil {
   106→				timeout = parsed
   107→			}
   108→		}
   109→		if timeout == 0 {
   110→			timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   111→		}
   112→	}
   113→
   114→	return &ClaudeCodeRunner{
   115→		Model:        model,
   116→		Timeout:      timeout,
   117→		AllowedTools: []string{"Edit", "Read", "Write", "Bash", "Glob", "Grep"},
   118→	}
   119→}
   120→
   121→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) Name() string {
   122→	return AgentNameClaudeCode
   123→}
   124→
   125→// IsAvailable checks if Claude CLI is installed and responds to --version.
   126→// Note: This does NOT verify authentication status. Claude Code uses OAuth
   127→// authentication (via `claude login`), not ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. If the CLI is
   128→// installed but not logged in, tests will fail at RunPrompt time.
   129→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   130→	// Check if claude CLI is in PATH
   131→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("claude"); err != nil {
   132→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   133→	}
   134→
   135→	// Check if claude is working (--version doesn't require auth)
   136→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   137→	defer cancel()
   138→
   139→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", "--version")
   140→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   141→		return false, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not working: %w", err)
   142→	}
   143→
   144→	return true, nil
   145→}
   146→
   147→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   148→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, r.AllowedTools)
   149→}
   150→
   151→func (r *ClaudeCodeRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   152→	// Build command: claude --model <model> -p "<prompt>" --allowedTools <tools>
   153→	args := []string{
   154→		"--model", r.Model,
   155→		"-p", prompt,
   156→	}
   157→
   158→	if len(tools) > 0 {
   159→		// Claude CLI expects each tool as a separate argument after --allowedTools
   160→		// e.g., --allowedTools Edit Read Bash (not --allowedTools "Edit,Read,Bash")
   161→		args = append(args, "--allowedTools")
   162→		args = append(args, tools...)
   163→	}
   164→
   165→	// Create context with timeout
   166→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, r.Timeout)
   167→	defer cancel()
   168→
   169→	//nolint:gosec // args are constructed from trusted config, not user input
   170→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", args...)
   171→	cmd.Dir = workDir
   172→
   173→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   174→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   175→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   176→
   177→	start := time.Now()
   178→	err := cmd.Run()
   179→	duration := time.Since(start)
   180→
   181→	result := &AgentResult{
   182→		Stdout:   stdout.String(),
   183→		Stderr:   stderr.String(),
   184→		Duration: duration,
   185→	}
   186→
   187→	if err != nil {
   188→		exitErr := &exec.ExitError{}
   189→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   190→			result.ExitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
   191→		} else {
   192→			result.ExitCode = -1
   193→		}
   194→		//nolint:wrapcheck // error is from exec.Run, caller can check ExitCode in result
   195→		return result, err
   196→	}
   197→
   198→	result.ExitCode = 0
   199→	return result, nil
   200→}
   201→
   202→// GeminiCLIRunner implements AgentRunner for Gemini CLI.
   203→// This is a placeholder for future implementation.
   204→type GeminiCLIRunner struct {
   205→	Timeout time.Duration
   206→}
   207→
   208→// NewGeminiCLIRunner creates a new Gemini CLI runner with the given config.
   209→func NewGeminiCLIRunner(config AgentRunnerConfig) *GeminiCLIRunner {
   210→	timeout := config.Timeout
   211→	if timeout == 0 {
   212→		timeout = 2 * time.Minute
   213→	}
   214→
   215→	return &GeminiCLIRunner{
   216→		Timeout: timeout,
   217→	}
   218→}
   219→
   220→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) Name() string {
   221→	return AgentNameGeminiCLI
   222→}
   223→
   224→// IsAvailable checks if Gemini CLI is installed and authenticated.
   225→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) IsAvailable() (bool, error) {
   226→	// Check if gemini CLI is in PATH
   227→	if _, err := exec.LookPath("gemini"); err != nil {
   228→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not found in PATH: %w", err)
   229→	}
   230→
   231→	// Check if gemini is working
   232→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   233→	defer cancel()
   234→
   235→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "gemini", "--version")
   236→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
   237→		return false, fmt.Errorf("gemini CLI not working: %w", err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	return true, nil
   241→}
   242→
   243→func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string) (*AgentResult, error) {
   244→	return r.RunPromptWithTools(ctx, workDir, prompt, nil)
   245→}
   246→

Based on the search results, here's what I found about Gemini CLI:

## Gemini CLI Overview

The Gemini command line interface (CLI) is an open source AI agent that provides access to Gemini directly in your terminal.(no content)The Gemini CLI uses a reason and act (ReAct) loop with your built-in tools and local or remote MCP servers to complete complex use cases like fixing bugs, creating new features, and improving test coverage.

### Key Features

- Free tier: 60 requests/min and 1,000 requests/day with personal Google account. Access to powerful Gemini 3 models with improved reasoning and 1M token context window.
- Built-in tools include Google Search grounding, file operations, shell commands, and web fetching. It's extensible with MCP (Model Context Protocol) support for custom integrations and is designed as terminal-first for developers who live in the command line.

### Capabilities

While the Gemini CLI excels at coding, it's also a versatile local utility that you can use for a wide range of tasks, from content generation and problem solving to deep research and task management.(no content)Gemini CLI integrates with your local environment. It can read and edit files, execute shell commands, and search the web, all while maintaining your project context.

### Installation

You can run it without installation using: `npx @google/gemini-cli`(no content)The Gemini CLI is available without additional setup in Cloud Shell or can be set up in other environments.

### Open Source & Licensing

Gemini CLI is fully open source (Apache 2.0), and the community is encouraged to report bugs and suggest features.(no content)Google has integrated Gemini CLI with Gemini Code Assist, so all developers — on free, Standard, and Enterprise Code Assist plans — get prompt-driven, AI-first coding. To use Gemini CLI free-of-charge, simply login with a personal Google account to get a free Gemini Code Assist license.

### IDE Integration

Gemini Code Assist agent mode in VS Code is powered by Gemini CLI and offers a subset of its functionality directly within the chat in your IDE.

# Gemini CLI Command-Line Usage

## Running Prompts Non-Interactively

The documentation shows this approach for scripting:

```bash
gemini -p "Explain the architecture of this codebase"
```

For structured output suitable for automation, use:

```bash
gemini -p "Your prompt here" --output-format json
```

Real-time event streaming is available via:

```bash
gemini -p "Run tests and deploy" --output-format stream-json
```

## Model Selection

Specify a particular model with the `-m` flag:

```bash
gemini -m gemini-2.5-flash
```

## Project Context and Directory Scope

Include multiple directories using:

```bash
gemini --include-directories ../lib,../docs
```

Start Gemini CLI in the current directory with:

```bash
gemini
```

## Tool Integration

The documentation emphasizes "MCP (Model Context Protocol) support for custom integrations" rather than listing specific tool-allowlist flags. Instead, tools are configured in `~/.gemini/settings.json` to extend capabilities through MCP servers.

The built-in tools include file system operations, shell commands, and web fetch functionality, but the content provided doesn't specify flags to selectively enable/disable individual tools at runtime.

Done. The Gemini CLI runner is now implemented. To run the e2e tests with Gemini:

```bash
# Run with Gemini CLI (default model: gemini-2.5-flash)
E2E_AGENT=gemini-cli go test -tags=e2e ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...

# Or specify a different model
E2E_AGENT=gemini-cli E2E_GEMINI_MODEL=gemini-2.5-pro go test -tags=e2e ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...

# Adjust timeout if needed
E2E_AGENT=gemini-cli E2E_TIMEOUT=5m go test -tags=e2e ./cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/...
```

**Note:** Unlike Claude Code, Gemini CLI doesn't have a `--allowedTools` flag to restrict tools at runtime. Tools are configured via `~/.gemini/settings.json` and MCP servers. The `tools` parameter in `RunPromptWithTools` is currently ignored for Gemini.

**Sources:**
- [Gemini CLI GitHub](https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli)
- [Gemini CLI Documentation](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/gemini-cli)

      --accept-raw-output-risk    Suppress the security warning when using --raw-output.  [boolean]
  -v, --version                   Show version number  [boolean]
  -h, --help                      Show help  [boolean]
```

1. `--yolo` or `--approval-mode yolo` to auto-approve tool use (needed for non-interactive)
2. `--allowed-tools` flag (like Claude!)
3. Also there's a config error in your local `.gemini/settings.json`

Let me update the runner to add the necessary flags:

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/e2e_test/agent_runner.go",
  "old_string": "func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, _ []string) (*AgentResult, error) {\n\t// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p \"<prompt>\"\n\t// Note: Gemini CLI doesn't have --allowedTools flag like Claude Code.\n\t// Tools are configured via ~/.gemini/settings.json and MCP servers.\n\t// The tools parameter is ignored for now.\n\targs := []string{\n\t\t\"-m\", r.Model,\n\t\t\"-p\", prompt,\n\t}",
  "new_string": "func (r *GeminiCLIRunner) RunPromptWithTools(ctx context.Context, workDir string, prompt string, tools []string) (*AgentResult, error) {\n\t// Build command: gemini -m <model> -p \"<prompt>\" --yolo --allowed-tools <tools>\n\t// --yolo auto-approves all tool use (required for non-interactive mode)\n\t// --allowed-tools restricts which tools can run without confirmation\n\targs := []string{\n\t\t\"-m\", r.Model,\n\t\t\"-p\", prompt,\n\t\t\"--yolo\", // Auto-approve all actions for non-interactive mode\n\t}\n\n\tif len(tools) > 0 {\n\t\targs = append(args, \"--allowed-tools\")\n\t\targs = append(args, tools...)\n\t}"
}

```
# Gemini CLI Command-Line Flags for Non-Interactive Mode

## 1. `--approval-mode` Flag

The `--approval-mode` flag controls how tool calls are handled. Available options:

- **`default`**: "Prompt for approval on each tool call (default behavior)"
- **`auto_edit`**: "Automatically approve edit tools (replace, write_file) while prompting for others"
- **`yolo`**: "Automatically approve all tool calls"

Key constraint: "Cannot be used together with `--yolo`" — use `--approval-mode=yolo` instead.

## 2. `--allowed-tools` Flag

Format uses comma-separated values in a single argument:

```
--allowed-tools "ShellTool(git status)"
```

The documentation specifies: "A comma-separated list of tool names that will bypass the confirmation dialog." Tool names can include command-specific restrictions (e.g., `ShellTool(ls -l)`).

## 3. Other Headless/Non-Interactive Relevant Flags

- **`--prompt`** (`-p`): "Used to pass a prompt directly to the command. This invokes Gemini CLI in a non-interactive mode."
- **`--yolo`**: Automatically approves all tool calls (disables confirmations entirely)
- **`--sandbox`** / **`-s`**: Enables sandboxing for safer execution

The `--prompt-interactive` flag explicitly cannot be used for headless execution, as it "Cannot be used when piping input from stdin."
```
